# DEC-031: Forward paper arena lane (many frozen bots on one live paper tape, each screened on the promotion gate)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Status** | **Proposed 2026-10-18; revision 2 applies quant-proof's edits A to K (PR #706 comment 6245150476).** It takes effect on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR. Nothing here allows a live send. Amendments 1 to 3 are in §5; Amendment 2 sets the deciding rent of §1.4 to 0 for the books it covers; Amendment 3 sets rolling registration, automatic confirmation of every screen pass, the multiplicity block of every read, October arena-tape screening, a second-copy ban, the canary prerequisites, the bank-pass confirmation and the list of taints. |
| **Decider** | Vaan (owner) set the direction in chat at about 03:00Z on 2026-10-18: "Start to come up with a plan, and then execute on it"; more SOL after "something like 5 days of forward testing before going live". The Claude manager runs the lane. |
| **Date** | 2026-10-18 |

## 0. Why this exists
Up to now each candidate has been certified one at a time on a sealed block. Edges are thin against their variance, so each read takes 2 to 3 weeks, and few lines are tested at once. This DEC adds a second lane. Many bots trade forward on paper at the same time from one live tape. Each bot is frozen before its own clock starts and read once, on the unchanged gate. A first pass is a screen, not a promotion (§2): scaling above a canary needs a corrected confirmation on later trades. The lane does not replace sealed reads.

## 1. The lane
1. **Tape.** One live tape, `/data/mal/arena/tape`, written by `tools/arena_feed.py` from public RPC. It covers PumpSwap and pump.fun events and spends 0 Helius credits. It is new data with its own path. It is not Forward walk 2 data. No arena process opens `/data/mal/blocks/forward-1016` or any path the ledger or the manager's seal list keeps closed. A book may read another live feed instead of this tape (for example an arena H5 book on mal-fast-0 reading its own public-RPC logsSubscribe stream through `tools/h5_shadow.py`, with the tip union off). Its `bots.json` line then names that feed and its pinned decoder blobs; that feed's own gap log (for `tools/h5_shadow.py`, its `gap` records) is its §1.4 gap source; and its parity set is in that feed's format. The H5 books run on mal-fast-0, a different host and filesystem from the walk-2 walker, and write their own small output there (§3.8, "unless it writes to a different filesystem").
2. **Book.** A book is a `bot_id`, a `frozen_sha` (the commit of all code and prompts it runs) and a `clock_start_ms`.
   - Before `clock_start_ms`, the book's line goes in `/data/mal/arena/bots.json` and is copied to a MiScusi notebook entry. The line holds: `bot_id`, `frozen_sha`, `clock_start_ms`, the deciding stake of §2, and the exact model IDs and sampling settings of any LLM it calls. `frozen_sha` is a commit on origin. A records PR within 24 h copies every line. No line is ever removed: a stopped book stays listed with its stop instant and counts in every multiplicity disclosure.
   - A counted decision is written live, at its decision instant and before its fill is priced. It is never generated, regenerated or replayed afterwards. Each book's decision-log sha256 goes into a MiScusi notebook entry at least once per UTC day.
   - Only trades with entry decision time at or after `clock_start_ms` count. Any code or prompt change makes a new `bot_id` with a new clock. The old book keeps running or stops; it is never merged with the new one.
3. **Self-improving books.** For a book whose own reviewer rewrites a playbook (Haiku trader v4):
   - the frozen part is the code, the reviewer's instructions and the model IDs;
   - each daily playbook version is hashed and logged before the first decision that uses it;
   - a version may use only outcomes whose exit is before its write instant;
   - the book is the whole system, versions included.

   Only the book's own reviewer, at the frozen code, instructions and model IDs, writes playbook versions. Any other edit makes a new `bot_id`.
4. **Costs and fills.** `tools/arena_paper.py` prices fills at the lab's deciding stack: 505,000 lamports per send, rent 2,039,280 lamports per filled trip, +26.08/−16 bps, PRE-trade PumpSwap reserves with V, fee tiers, own impact, flat 15% and pressure-fail legs, and 1.3 s / 1.9 s latency legs. It must match the stack to the lamport on a committed parity set before any book's clock starts.
   - **E1 floor.** Per filled trip, the stack also carries the larger of the haircut and 384,022 lamports per 0.05 SOL of stake. This is the pre-seal n = 8 live-minus-sim figure (DEC-021:54), scaled linearly as in DEC-021 Amendment 7 option B.
   - **Exit lag:** ceil(0.55 s / slot_s) slots, at least 2.
   - **Strand class:** an unpriced or invalid exit, or proceeds below one send, is a total loss of stake plus the buy send and rent. It is never dropped.
   - **Latency start:** the latency legs start at the decision instant (when the decision record is written, mapped to the tape's slot clock), never at the triggering print.
   - **Feed gaps:** a trade whose decision-to-exit span holds a feed gap the feed logged (a disconnect or resubscribe), or a print the stack needs without V, is excluded by that rule alone and counted. If more than 5% of a book's trades are excluded this way at its read, the read is NOT_DECIDABLE.
   - **Pins:** these go in the records PR before the first clock:
     - the parity reference (module path@sha256);
     - the parity set (exploration-pool hours in the arena tape's format, path@sha256);
     - the sha256 of `tools/arena_feed.py`, `tools/arena_paper.py` and `tools/arena_score.py`.

     Any later change to them is disclosed in every read it touches, with quant-proof's OK on the change.
5. **Stakes.** One book's decisions are also priced at 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0 SOL (H5: 0.10, 0.25, 0.30), with impact at each size. Only the stake pinned in `bots.json` decides (§2).

## 2. Gate read
- **One read per `bot_id`, at an outcome-blind instant.**
  - **When:** the first 00:00Z boundary at which the book has at least 100 counted attempts (every entry decision, guarded reverts included) over at least 5 UTC dates. The count uses decision timestamps only, by a tool that loads no fill, exit or P&L.
  - **What it covers:** the attempts decided before that boundary, once all have exited. Nothing else starts, delays or repeats the read. A FAIL or NOT_DECIDABLE is final for that `bot_id`.
  - **The test:** CLAUDE.md's promotion gate, unchanged:
    - a majority of days positive;
    - the CI90 lower bound of mean SOL per trade > 0 (1,000 bootstrap draws, seed 1, 5th percentile);
    - total positive after removing the top 3 trades;
    - all of this under both the flat 15% and the pressure-fail model.
  - DEC-014 Amendments 1 and 2 apply: an unresolved restart orphan, a failed settlement, or a counted trade without its pressure-leg P&L makes the read NOT_DECIDABLE.
- **Deciding cell:** the 1.9 s leg at the stake pinned in `bots.json` before the clock (§1.2). The 1.3 s leg and the other stakes are reported beside it and never decide.
- Ex-best-day is reported. A book whose total is not positive ex-best-day is not proposed for a canary.
- **Multiplicity.** Every read states the number of arena books in `bots.json` with a clock before the read (stopped ones included), their cells, and how many were read. A first pass is a **screen**: the best of that many, in-sample for selection, not a promotion. It allows only steps 1 to 3 below, with the canary an execution measurement at its DEC's small limits.
- **A pass is not a profit claim.** The path after a screen pass:
  1. quant-proof checks the read on its exact files;
  2. the owner's OK;
  3. a live canary under its own DEC, with small limits;
  4. **stake above the canary's limits** needs a **confirmation**. The same `bot_id`, unchanged, must clear the same gate on its deciding cell over attempts decided after the screen read's instant: at least 100 attempts on at least 5 further UTC dates, read once at the outcome-blind instant above. **And** its one-sided bootstrap p (10,000 draws, seed 1; the larger p of the two fail models) must be at most 0.05/c. Here c is the number of arena books that have entered a confirmation under this DEC by that read, failed and NOT_DECIDABLE ones included (DEC-014 (b); Bonferroni for staggered reads, as in DEC-030 §1). Live fills must also match paper. Parity is required in addition to the confirmation and never replaces it.
- No retune on a book's own counted days. A retuned rule is a new book.

## 3. Isolation from EXP-027 and the sealed reads
1. Until EXP-027's read is written, no arena book uses, and no arena process opens, any of these:
   - the r4-b boosters (`models/r4b/`);
   - any model fitted on the 63 features of r4-b's `features.txt` (the S1 / l3 LightGBM line, any seed or variant);
   - `tools/r4b_*.py` and `02_build.py`;
   - `/data/mal/r4b/`;
   - r4-b's development outputs under `/data/mal/hunt-1008/laya-opt/`.
2. Arena results never change anything in EXP-027. Its window, stop rule, k and read are fixed by its registration.
3. Arena books are not push-family books. They take no DEC-030 slot and do not change EXP-027's k. Arena results are walk-2-hour information under DEC-030 §2 R1 ("a book's design must not use them"). No book designed, tuned or selected with any arena result registers as a push-family book on walk 2.
4. After EXP-027's read, the manager records the following in the ledger's EXP-027 sub-entry and beside the read's report, never in EXP-027's file (its Window record takes only its closed key list, EXP-027 §0.5):
   - the arena's existence;
   - the number of arena books;
   - the count of arena trades, paper and any live canary send, on pools that r4-b held or attempted in the same hour. The count is computed after the read.
5. **H5 seals.**
   - The CAP-PICK seal is moot. EXP-022 was withdrawn before counting on 2026-10-15, which is an end state, so EXP-022's read has ended (EXP-024 §3, "to the end of EXP-022's read").
   - The H5 Look-2 seal is not moot. EXP-024 §3's seal and Amendment 4 item D1 stay in force until EXP-024's end state, the first listed being 2026-11-14T00:00Z (EXP-024 A3 halt record; DEC-030 §3). They cover H5 outcomes on any October hour of a V-range pool in its first 360 s, from any source, RPC and paper runners included.
   - **v1 is covered by EXP-024 Amendment 2.** An arena H5 v1 book whose decisions come from `tools/h5_shadow.py`'s decision path (md5 decision-equivalence on the default path), run keyless with no orders, is the shadow detector of Amendment 2. Its outcomes for pools with s0 in `[2026-10-16T00, 2026-11-06T00)` are under that declared observation.
   - **v2 is not covered by Amendment 2** (EXP-024 Amendment 8 (e)). An arena v2 book counts only pools with s0 at or after 2026-11-06T00, unless a dated EXP-024 record declaring the real-time observation of its outcomes merges with quant-proof's OK before its clock starts. EXP-024 Amendment 9, proposed in the same PR as this DEC, is that record.
   - **Synthetic pools.** Before 2026-11-14T00:00Z no arena H5 record carries an outcome for a synthetic pool or splits an outcome by class. The book's universe excludes synthetic and unclassified pools before any outcome exists, as EXP-024 Amendment 4 A2 and B3 do (Amendment 4 A3 and D1).
   - An arena H5 book counts only pools with s0 at or after its clock start. It never opens the outputs of shadow job #817 or the live canary.
6. The domain seals D2, D3 and D4 of the Forward walk row cover the hours `[2026-10-02T10, 2026-10-16T01)` and the tip tape. The arena opens no hour of `[2026-10-02T10, 2026-10-16T01)` from any source, the tip tape included; a book that reads another live feed uses only data from its own clock on.
7. **C1-NF (EXP-025).** EXP-025 §4's seal covers C1-NF labels, fills, exits and P&L on any October row from any source, RPC and paper runners included. The seal on `[2026-10-10T00, 2026-10-24T02)` holds until EXP-025's end state "Look 2 refused" is recorded on main, which is not before the R8 run after 2026-10-26T12:00:00Z (EXP-025 P5 record). Until then:
   - no arena book is, or reproduces, the C1-NF book or its pass-A labels (C1 `RULE.md`, `ARTIFACTS/c1nf_model/`);
   - no arena process opens the outputs of jobs #658 or #662.
8. **Walk 2 keeps its room.** No arena write takes free space on the walk-2 walker's filesystem below 25%. The walker stops below its 20% reserve, and PLAN.md stream E records walk 2 stalled for that reason. The feed checks before each hourly write and stops, logging a gap, rather than write below 25%, unless it writes to a different filesystem.

## 4. Not allowed under this DEC
- Any live send.
- Any read of a sealed path.
- Any change to a running job's command.
- Counting a trade decided before its book's clock.
- Opening the outputs of jobs #817, #729, #658 or #662, or of the H5 executor.

## 5. Amendments

### Amendment 1 (written 2026-10-19T03:31:35Z, after the H5 books' clock of 2026-10-18T06:00:00Z and before any read instant)
DEC-031 Amendment 1 (before any H5 arena read instant; outcome-blind). For h5v1-boostfloor-a1 and h5v2-boostclock-a1, a pv trigger record for which `tools.h5_executor.parse_shadow_trigger(row, "pv", None)` at blob 03995837317f returns a `bad_intent` refusal is the frozen bot's own pre-send refusal. It is not an entry decision. It is not an attempt for §2's count or read instant, and it is never priced. Its count by reason is reported beside every count and the read. A `bad_intent:missing_*` result is a schema fault and stops the scorer. Every other exclusion stays under the 5% cap, computed over non-refused attempts. Only the counts of jobs #1175 and #1176 were in view. No arena H5 outcome, fill, exit, strip, ledger row or P&L was opened. Code, frozen sha, clock, window, stake, deciding cell and gate are unchanged. `tools/arena_h5_score.py` changes from sha256 a3dd6b31c38081b535cb965223623ae5797341b7374b0d3b3196799e79e65c27 to dfe7f018601633921f06668fd1af3aaff7b87bf00f321d1540e10b41f8ab6006.

Disclosure (added before merge). After the paragraph above was written at 2026-10-19T03:31:35Z, and before it merged, the counts-only dry run of job #1191 (2026-10-19T03:43:26Z, scorer at 49fd6c5, the quant-proof pre-merge condition) was seen. For each book it showed 23 triggers seen, 12 refused (all `bad_intent:base_breaks_unresolved_settled`), 11 attempts, 11 counted, 0 excluded, 0 open, and not_decidable false. The merge was decided with those counts in view. No rule text changed after them, and no arena H5 outcome, fill, exit, strip, ledger row or P&L was opened. The disclosure instant in the read sentence stays 2026-10-19T03:31:35Z, the instant the rule was fixed.

Bias note. Missed prints rise with print count and feed load, so the kept (non-refused) set tilts toward quieter pools and quieter periods; the sign of that effect on P&L is unknown. The refusal share also moved in the counts seen: 9 of 13 triggers in the first count, then 3 of the next 10. With `tipauth=None` pinned, any integer `base_breaks_unresolved_settled` of 1 or more is a refusal.

### Amendment 2 (written 2026-10-19T17:27:15Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0; revised 2026-10-19T17:40:45Z after the quant-proof and reviewer comments on PR #740 at 94e2ed6; before the clock of any book it covers and before any arena read instant; outcome-blind)
DEC-031 Amendment 2: the arena's deciding rent is refunded. For the books in item 2 it is 0 lamports on every trip whose leg state is `filled` (the selector of item 7). This turns quant-proof's ruling R1 of 2026-10-19 into DEC text and does not change the ruling's substance. Where quant-proof's comment 6273356030 on PR #740 corrects the ruling's own R1 (e), (f), (g) and (h) wording, this text follows the comment (items 1, 6, 7, 8 and 10).
- Ruling, pinned in the repo: `ARTIFACTS/lab/qp-ruling-1019-cohort2.md` (sha256 `2288b71cbf1c315c46b6f1f111a1a3e6aa2972a27d9c093a79113a0cdc423ea9`). It is `/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt21/QP-RULING-1019-cohort2.md` as last modified 2026-10-19T17:36:28Z (sha256 `1583c4d7a8b8194bd60356dd027bbc436d56c8250617a1a17543b41dd31cb8cc`) with a header note added. The `/data` file is mutable; the repo copy is the cited text.
- The version quant-proof checked at 94e2ed6 had sha256 `c6f0a5e3ed022b6aa319238738e97a245d681de7daa138bb08abad342dbefe81` (mtime 2026-10-19T17:23:17Z). R1 and R8 item 7 were re-read in the later version and are unchanged. Sections R9 and R9(f) are not used here.
- Items 1 to 11 carry its R1 conditions (a) and (c) to (h) and its R8 item 7; item 12 carries condition (b); item 13 carries its basis.
- Like the rest of DEC-031 it takes effect on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR, and it must merge before the first clock it touches. A config ruling alone is not enough: `tools/arena_paper.py`'s docstring cannot override merged DEC text, and §1.4 names 2,039,280 lamports per filled trip.

1. **What changes.** §1.4 prices rent at 2,039,280 lamports per filled trip "at the lab's deciding stack". For a covered book the deciding rent is 0 on every trip in state `filled` (item 7). Everything else in §1.4 is unchanged: 505,000 lamports per send, +26.08/-16 bps, PRE-trade PumpSwap reserves with V, fee tiers, own impact, the flat 15% and pressure-fail legs, the latency legs, exit lag, the E1 floor (the larger of the haircut and 384,022 lamports per 0.05 SOL of stake, a max and not a sum), the strand class and the feed-gap rule.
2. **Scope.** The amendment covers every book whose `clock_start_ms` and whose registration (§1.2: its `bots.json` line and notebook copy) are both after the merge. The merge instant is the committer time of the squash-merge commit on origin/main. The rule is uniform. No book chooses its rent.
   - **HV4** is covered only if, before its registration, both of these hold:
     - (i) its host, `tools/hv4_runner.py`, passes `rent=0` explicitly. At origin/main 77bdf7e, line 1595 builds `stack_config(feed_delay_slots=...)` with the default, so HV4 is rent-charged as built;
     - (ii) its frozen code enforces item 6's pool filter. At 77bdf7e, `tools/hv4_cards.py` `filters` (lines 399-400) checks PumpSwap and a WSOL quote only and has no V band, so a rent-0 HV4 as built could trade non-canonical WSOL pools.
   - Coverage is tied to HV4's registered `clock_start_ms`: that and its registration must both be after the merge. T0 is not fixed here. R1 named 2026-10-21T00:00Z, which is only the earliest. T0 is the first 00:00Z after a clean 24 h stub run at the final sha and the registration merge, and was expected to be 2026-10-22T00:00Z when this text was revised.
   - Items 5 to 9 apply to HV4 like any covered book.
   - (i) and (ii) are code changes before HV4's freeze and are not part of this amendment's PR. If either does not happen, HV4 decides at rent-charged as built and this amendment covers the cohort-2 books only.
3. **Where rent 0 is set.** Each covered host passes `rent=0` in its `StackConfig`. `tools/arena_paper.py`'s default (`RENT_LAMPORTS`, 2,039,280) is unchanged, and so is its sha256, which is a §1.4 pin.
4. **Strand rent stays 2,039,280.** A strand (an unpriced or invalid exit, or proceeds below one send) still loses stake, the buy send and 2,039,280 (`F.RENT_STRANDED`), and is never dropped. It is not re-based down to chain rent. Disclosed: 2,039,280 is 34,800 below EXP-027's Token-2022 convention (2,074,080) and 525,440 above the chain's 1,513,840 (2026-10-16, 170-byte Token-2022 ATA). One-off accounts such as the PumpSwap `user_volume_accumulator` are paid once per wallet, not per trip. They are disclosed here and are not charged per trip.
5. **Canary carry-over (EXP-027 condition (i) mirrored).** A live canary from a rent-0 book must sell through a builder that sells and closes the base ATA in one transaction under the mint's token program, pinned as path@sha256 (today `tools/h5_executor.py` and `tools/probe_live.py` do). Otherwise the rent-charged leg of item 7 is the paper comparator for that canary.
6. **Pool universe and close-blocking class (EXP-027 (iii) and item 4 mirrored).**
   - **Universe.** A covered book trades only canonical pump pools: quote_mint == WSOL (read before the V test) and V in [17.5e9, 17.7e9] on the deciding row (QP ruling R2 item 3 and R9 (b)). Each covered book's own frozen code enforces this at decision time, so a covered book has no out-of-band trip to price. For the cohort-2 books it is a registration precondition. The records PR names the check as path:line@frozen_sha.
   - **Fetch.** After the read boundary, one structure-only mint-account fetch of the book's traded mints is run. It records counts, and the credits it uses are recorded.
   - **Retries.** A transport failure is retried up to 3 times, all within 24 h of the first attempt, by the same rule for every mint. A mint that still fails then is a failed fetch. The fetch reads current chain state, so a freeze authority revoked after the trade is missed. This is disclosed, not corrected.
   - **Close-blocked trip.** A freeze authority, an extension outside {metadataPointer, tokenMetadata}, or a failed fetch makes that trip close-blocked in every sub-cell. It is priced the way `arena_stack.round_trip` prices a caller-stranded trip (`stranded=True`): pnl_nofail = -stake - send - 2,039,280. Under "total" it bypasses the fail blend. Under "sc" it is blended the way "sc" blends `entry_invalid` and `exit_invalid` rows. Its `filled` flag stays True, so p_i and the pressure refit do not change. The reclassification is applied before item 7, and a close-blocked trip leaves state `filled`.
   - This is pre-declared here. Census prior (EXP-027 allowlist census): 295 of 300 Token-2022 with exactly those two extensions, 5 SPL, 0 freeze.
7. **Rent-charged leg beside every deciding figure.** Every read of a covered book prints the rent-charged leg beside each deciding figure, as an exact transform of the same rows. The selector is the leg state, not the `filled` and `strand` flags: a trip i is transformed under a strand rule only if `calc[lat].by_rule[rule].state == "filled"`, after item 6's reclassification. For those trips:
   - flat leg: minus 0.85 x 2,039,280 lamports;
   - pressure leg: minus (1 - p_i) x 2,039,280 lamports, with p_i the cell's own refit probability for that trip.

   Rows in state `guarded`, `stranded`, `entry_invalid` or `exit_invalid` are unchanged under both rules. Under "sc", `entry_invalid` and `exit_invalid` rows have `filled` True and `strand` False, but their pnl already carries 2,039,280 of strand rent whatever `cfg.rent` is, so selecting on the flags would charge them twice. The transform is computed with a pinned report tool, or with a scorer change that carries quant-proof's OK and has its sha256 disclosed (§1.4 pins). Any pass sentence quotes it. A book that passes only at rent 0 is described in those words.
8. **Config pin and read check.** The records PR pins each covered book's `StackConfig`: `rent` 0, `e1_floor_per_005` 384022, `strand_rule` "both", `k_rounding`, `feed_delay_slots`, `gap_slots` 150 and `gap_silence_ms` 60000.
   - **Check.** The read compares every key of `StackConfig.as_dict()` on every engine start row and every open row with the pin. It is counts-only and runs before any outcome of the book is opened. Per-trade `sps_entry` and `sps_exit` on open rows are ignored, as `CONFIG_KEYS_IGNORED` ignores them. A mismatch on any pinned field makes the read NOT_DECIDABLE, and that is final for the `bot_id`. Nothing is re-priced, and item 7's transform is not used to fall back to the pin. `tools/arena_score.py` refuses only MIXED configs inside a bot (lines 300-302) and never checks values, so without this check a wrong config would pass silently.
   - **The two gap fields.** R1 (g) implied that `as_dict()` emits `gap_slots` and `gap_silence_ms`. At 77bdf7e it does not (it emits `rent`, `e1_floor_per_005`, `strand_rule`, `k_rounding` and `feed_delay_slots`, among others), as quant-proof's comment 6273356030 states. Their pin is therefore static, from the host's `StackConfig` call at the host's pinned frozen sha. It binds only if all three of these hold:
     - (i) the scorer ties every counted open row to a clean engine start at the frozen commit (`tools/arena_score.py` lines 287-296);
     - (ii) `StackConfig` is frozen (`frozen=True`);
     - (iii) no runtime input (CLI flag, env var, config file, `**kw` passthrough) reaches either field.
   - **Records PR.** It states (iii). It also states either that the host's `StackConfig` call at frozen_sha passes neither field (the defaults of 150 and 60,000 in `tools/arena_paper.py` apply, at its §1.4-pinned sha256) or that it passes them as literals. If a runtime input reaches either field, the book is not registered until the engine logs the effective values.
9. **Read statement (QP R8 item 7).** Every arena read, covered or not, states the rent-charged and rent-0 legs side by side, which one decides, and why. For a covered book, rent 0 decides and the reason is this amendment. For an H5 book, rent-charged decides (item 10).
10. **H5 books are unchanged.** h5v1-boostfloor-a1 and h5v2-boostclock-a1 (clock 2026-10-18T06:00:00Z) keep deciding rent 2,039,280 (`tools/arena_h5_cost.py` `RENT`). Every H5 read discloses this amendment. It may print a rent-0 leg: +0.85 x 2,039,280 lamports on the flat leg and +(1 - p_i) x 2,039,280 lamports on the pressure leg, on exactly the trips item 7's selector picks (`by_rule[rule].state == "filled"`), labelled "report-only, added after the clock, never deciding". The H5 leg must not credit `entry_invalid` or `exit_invalid` rows under "sc". A post-clock switch in the known-favourable direction is a researcher degree of freedom. That is why it is not allowed, even though rent 0 is the truer cost.
11. **No retroactive switch.** If this amendment does not merge before a book's clock or registration, that book decides at rent-charged as built.
12. **Outcome-blind statement (QP R1 (b)).** The shift is deterministic and per trade, and the ruling rests on code and pre-arena evidence. R1 (b) asks which arena, HV4-shakedown or replay P&L figures the chooser had in view.
    - **The manager's statement**, recorded in MiScusi notebook entry n_x_CjdirQZfaC5w, with the supplement entry n_bg2-0svirXsC_g that the manager added to the notebook after the quant-proof comment:
      - the manager has seen no arena P&L of any book;
      - H5 arena books: counts only (dry-run counts; no fills or P&L opened);
      - the Haiku v4 shakedowns ran the StubModel (every answer abstains, so no trades), and the manager read cards, decision-log and timing counts only, never the engine ledger;
      - the cohort-2 scope and the H2 split used Aug/Sep exploration outputs priced at the old 55,000-lamport stack, not arena outputs;
      - the ruling rests on code (`tools/h5_executor.py:2524-2525`, `tools/probe_live.py:759-760`) and pre-arena evidence (EXP-027 §5.2 / QP-FU §1a, 2026-10-16).
    - **Replay P&L figures in view of the chooser when choosing:**
      - `COHORT2-SCOPE.md` (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt21/`, sha256 `3b9ef3182f972d3dc2184295423dce59894b550dfd893bd1071f8c61d8432190`). It holds Aug/Sep replay figures at the 55,000-lamport, no-rent stack and the scope's own estimates at the arena stack. Its line 6: "Every non-H5 survivor was +1.6% to +4.5% at 55,000. They land near zero or below" (extra drag "about 1.9 pp of stake at 0.25 SOL"). Its table: H2-PS "about 0 to +0.5%", G4 "about 0.0 / -0.3 after drag" (both "my arithmetic" in the scope).
      - `COUNTS-1019.md` (same directory, sha256 `0db2ccafc31a5a9794591b652170ab52c33a45bfcb19a457a4daceeb11cfc368`, generated 2026-10-19T17:18Z). Its §B1 holds Book A's September P&L by venue, including the PumpSwap subset in h2ps-a1's own deciding cell (1.9 s, 0.25 SOL): 528 attempts, +1.70% flat and +1.25% pressure per attempt, on the 55,000-lamport no-rent stack, in-sample. It was in view of the chooser. Quant-proof did not have it for R1.
    - **Direction.** The switch was adopted knowing that it favours the books about to be registered. The rent term is 0.816 pp of stake at 0.25 SOL, which is about 0.8 of the scope's ~1.9 pp extra arena drag at 0.25 (quant-proof's check of the scope's arithmetic, not a measurement), and it is about what decides the sign of their Aug/Sep figures at the arena stack. The ruling stands because the switch is uniform, comes before every covered clock and registration, and rests on code. Item 7's rent-charged leg is the guard.
    - The ruling's own header adds that quant-proof opened no arena tape, ledger, `bots.json`, shakedown directory, sealed path, tip tape or shadow, canary, probe or walk output, and that its probe figures (61 of 61 refunds, 1,513,840 chain rent) come from EXP-027 §5.2's pinned text.
13. **Basis (code and pre-arena evidence only).**
    - Both live sell paths put the sell and the base-ATA close in one transaction under the mint's own token program: `tools/h5_executor.py:2524-2525` and `tools/probe_live.py:759-760`. `tools/pumpswap_tx.sell_instructions` on its own closes only the WSOL ATA, so a builder that used it without the extra close would leave the base-ATA rent locked. Item 5 guards that.
    - The close is atomic with the sell. If it cannot run (tokens left behind, a close-blocking extension), the whole sell reverts and the position sticks. That is strand behaviour, not rent lost on a good sell.
    - Probe evidence: 61 of 61 probe trips refunded, net 0 (EXP-027 §5.2, QP-FU §1a). Chain rent on 2026-10-16 was 1,513,840, so 2,039,280 is 35% above what the chain charges even when rent is lost.
    - The lab's other deciding stack already prices rent at 0 (`arena_stack.RENT_DECIDING`, vendored from `r4b_frozen`; `EXP027_RENT_DECIDING` under EXP-027 §5.2 (i) to (iii)). §1.4 says "at the lab's deciding stack" and then lists 2,039,280; the two statements disagreed.
    - The E1 floor absorbs no rent in either direction. `probe_sim_calibration.pnl_of` builds the sim P&L as proceeds minus spend minus the live fees of both sides minus the failed-sell cost minus the same trip's net rent (rent charged minus rent refunded), and the live P&L contains that same net rent. Rent and fees cancel in live minus sim by construction, so the 384,022 per 0.05 SOL gap (n = 8) has no rent in it. Rent 0 drops no cost that E1 absorbed, and rent-charged does not double count with E1. The floor and the haircut stay a max.
    - Rent changes no other term of the deciding stack. The auto-strand threshold (proceeds below one send), the E1 extra (pnl0 minus pnl, where rent cancels) and the pressure refit (ssb, nearby, filled) do not depend on it. With item 7's selector, that makes the transform exact.
    - Size, quant-proof's arithmetic and not a measurement: 2,039,280 lamports per filled trip is 0.816% of stake at 0.25 SOL, 0.408% at 0.5 and 0.204% at 1.0. That is about 0.8 of the scope's ~1.9 pp extra arena drag at 0.25. Keeping a cost the executor does not pay is a mislabelled haircut. Margin the lab wants belongs in a named haircut, not in rent.
    - What rent 0 must not hide: a strand (item 4), a close-blocking mint (item 6), a canary through a builder without the in-tx close (item 5) and one-off accounts (item 4).

Unchanged by this amendment: the promotion gate and its thresholds; §2's deciding cell (1.9 s at the stake pinned in `bots.json`, flat and pressure, strand rules sc and total as in the ruling's R8 item 2) and its one read per `bot_id`; the E1 floor; the H5 books; every clock; §3; Amendment 1. A covered book that passes is still a screen (§2), not a profit claim.

### Amendment 3 (written 2026-10-19T19:46:30Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0; revised 2026-10-19T19:49:03Z to carry the ruling's Version 2, which was added to the ruling file after the first text was written; revised again 2026-10-19T19:58:16Z after quant-proof's comment 6276010033 on PR #751 at 2d2b50b; before the clock of any hunt23 book and before any arena read it touches; outcome-blind)
DEC-031 Amendment 3: books register one at a time, every screen pass enters confirmation, every read carries a multiplicity block, and the lane's rules for October arena-tape screening, second copies, the canary, a bank pass and the taint list are fixed. This turns quant-proof's throughput ruling of 2026-10-19, version 1 and Version 2 (items 1, 3, 5 and 6, and the arena side of item 4), into DEC text and does not change the ruling's substance. No arena tape, ledger, `bots.json` line, outcome, fill, exit or P&L was opened to write it.
- Ruling, pinned in the repo: `ARTIFACTS/lab/qp-ruling-1019-throughput.md` (sha256 `b482a70abd5956d65ea88940e3a4283bad6c2ed4f1a7320ecb233fd1cc840d78`). It is `/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt23/QP-RULING-1019-throughput.md` as last modified 2026-10-19T19:46:36Z (sha256 `79cea8998a6e45aaddfed9dd36987c9af346b1463847b3a1b507c5511450565d`; version 1 and "Version 2") with a header note added. The `/data` file is mutable; the repo copy is the cited text. The first text of this amendment pinned version 1 (mtime 2026-10-19T19:43:15Z, sha256 `2281fd1c1c077702effb1c44b05641c169cd2e9e59893bf25764ec020da19a75`); Version 2 changes version 1 as items 3, 7 and 8 below record. "R4", "R5", "R6", "R7", "R8" and "R9" below are sections of the cohort-2 ruling, pinned at `ARTIFACTS/lab/qp-ruling-1019-cohort2.md` and cited in Amendment 2.
- RULES.md, pinned by hash. `/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt23/RULES.md` is a mutable `/data` file. Its Amendment 1 is pinned as written 2026-10-19T19:42:30Z: sha256 `89908ae4a4bb0f09d3d828aa76d4356567c3919d52350f287f9a32c5380c06ba`, MiScusi notebook entry n_MdoisIU6opW1rw. It carries the ruling's item 2(a) to 2(e) (its items 1 and 2), the 360-s, C1-NF and wallet-history seals (its item 3), the declaration rule (its item 4), the bank exclusion for the first wave (its item 5) and "structure counts only" for October (its item 6). It carries no 2(f). Its line 46 (a cell under 20 attempts a day on explore-0814 is dropped) is an August pricing filter, not the October count. It was written before version 1 of the ruling was saved (19:43:15Z) and before Version 2 (19:46:36Z).
- Items 1 to 3 carry the throughput ruling's item 1 (with Version 2's report line), item 4 its item 3, items 5 and 6 its item 5, item 7 the arena side of its item 4 as Version 2 fixes it, and item 8 its item 6. The ruling's item 1, item 3 and item 6 are pinned here before the first hunt23 clock, as Version 2 requires. Its item 2 is split: 2(a) to 2(e) are applied for hunt23 by RULES.md Amendment 1 above and are not DEC text; 2(f), the structure-only October count as Version 2 states it, is carried here, in item 1 ("P0 October count"), because no other text carries it. The bank's own design (the rest of its item 4) is not DEC text (see the last paragraph).
- Like the rest of DEC-031 it takes effect on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR, and it must merge before the first hunt23 clock (hunt23 is the 2026-10-19 meme-coin push; its files are under `/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt23/`). The merge instant is the committer time of the squash-merge commit on origin/main, as in Amendment 2 item 2. No hunt23 book has a `clock_start_ms` earlier than it.

1. **Rolling registration** (ruling item 1). A book registers on its own and has its own clock. The clock needs no cohort. This is what §1.2 and §2 already give (each `bot_id` has its own clock and one read); this item states it for books that come one at a time.
   - **Clock** (new text: §1.2 and §2 leave the choice of `clock_start_ms` open, and this removes the choice). The registration record is the book's `bots.json` line and its notebook copy (§1.2). The book's `clock_start_ms` is the first 00:00Z after that record. Item 4, or another rule of this DEC, can put it later. It is never earlier.
   - **P0 October count** (ruling item 2(f), as Version 2 states it; every hunt23 book). Before a hunt23 book is registered, a structure-only October count on the arena tape is run, in the form of R3 of the cohort-2 ruling:
     - **Fixed first.** The rule text is written in a MiScusi notebook entry, with its instant, before the counting job starts, and the count has not been seen. The text names the window (whole closed UTC days of arena tape), the tool and the outputs.
     - **Structure only.** The tool is the book's rule module at its registration-candidate sha, counts-only: its entry predicate applied to type, prog, side, trader, mint or pool, quote_mint, virtual reserves, slot and receive time. No price, no engine run, no exit, no outcome. The outputs are would-be attempts per UTC day, and the feed gap counts in the window.
     - **Drop rule.** The cell is dropped before registration if it has fewer than 20 would-be attempts a day over the window, or if any day has fewer than 10. (Version 1 phrased this as at least 60 attempts over 3 days and no day under 10.) A hard gap, or a PumpSwap data_loss gap longer than 1 h in total, in the window makes the count NOT_DECIDABLE and the book is not registered on it.
     - **Record.** The counts, the job id and the output sha256 go in the notebook. A dropped book never enters `bots.json`. It is disclosed and not counted in N (item 3).
   - **Order.** The `bots.json` line and its notebook copy go in before the clock (§1.2).
   - **Records PR.** The records PR follows within 24 h (§1.2). It copies every line. No line is ever removed.
   - **One process per `frozen_sha`** (R5(a)). A host process runs from a clean checkout at exactly its books' `frozen_sha` and hosts only books that share that sha. A later book on another sha is a new process, never the old process restarted on a newer checkout.
   - **V-band check.** For a covered book (Amendment 2 item 2), the records PR names the check of Amendment 2 item 6 (quote_mint == WSOL, read before the V test, and V in [17.5e9, 17.7e9] on the deciding row) as `path:line@frozen_sha`.
   - **Family-wise rule.** §2's two stages stay: the screen, then the confirmation on a fresh window at 0.05/c. The confirmation is the correction. No Bonferroni over N is added at the screen.
2. **Automatic confirmation** (ruling item 1, the one gap). Every screen PASS enters §2 step 4's confirmation automatically, at its read.
   - **What a pass enters.** A screen pass is a read of a `bot_id` that clears §2's gate on its deciding cell. It enters the confirmation of §2 step 4: the same `bot_id`, unchanged, with at least 100 attempts on at least 5 further UTC dates decided after the screen read's instant, read once at §2's outcome-blind instant, and a one-sided bootstrap p (10,000 draws, seed 1; the larger p of the two fail models) of at most 0.05/c.
   - **c.** Every pass counts in c. c counts confirmation tests: the number of arena books that have entered a confirmation by that read, failed and NOT_DECIDABLE ones included (§2 step 4), one test each, except that item 7 gives a bank-pass book up to two. Entry is the screen pass itself. It does not wait for a request for a canary or for a stake above one.
   - **c is a running count.** For staggered reads, an early confirmation is held to a looser 0.05/c than a later one (§2 as merged, not changed here). Each confirmation read states its c and says so.
   - **Nobody chooses.** Nobody chooses which passes are confirmed or counted: not the manager, the owner, quant-proof or the book's agent. A pass is not left out of c because nobody asked for its confirmation. A stopped book that passed stays listed and counted (§1.2).
3. **The R8 multiplicity block** (ruling item 1). Every arena read carries this block. It sits beside §2's Multiplicity paragraph, R8 of the cohort-2 ruling and Amendment 2 item 9, and replaces none of them. It states:
   - **N.** Every book with a clock before the read, stopped and NOT_DECIDABLE books included, plus the verdicts of the books already read.
   - **Per book.** Its agent; the DECLARE sha256 and notebook id; the cells declared, priced, killed and surviving; its blocks; and any October hours used.
   - **Cells in total.** The total cell count across hunt23, J3 and cohort 2. Books dropped at P0 or at the bank are disclosed but not counted in N. (J3 is the arena-only book of R9. Cohort 2 is R8 item 1's list. The bank is the fresh-08 holdout bank of the ruling's item 4.)
   - **p.** The one-sided p (10,000 draws, seed 1, the larger of the two fail models).
   - **The plain line.** If no book has an edge, the chance of at least one screen pass is up to 1 - 0.95^N. That is 40% at N = 10 and 79% at N = 30.
   - **Screen-read line** (Version 2, item 1). Each screen read also reports, report-only, its p (10,000 draws, seed 1, the larger of the two fail models) and its Holm rank among all arena reads (those made by that read). Neither decides on a screen read. (Item 7 uses the same p as the confirmation statistic of a bank-pass book's first read.) The screen read itself stays uncorrected and buys only §2 steps 1 to 3.
4. **Screening on October arena-tape hours** (ruling item 3). A cohort's rules may be screened on closed hours of the arena tape, under these conditions:
   - **Cohort** (quant-proof's text on PR #751; the ruling uses the word without defining it). A cohort is a closed list of books, each with its DECLARE sha256 and notebook id. The list is written in a notebook entry before the first price of any listed book on any block, Aug/Sep or October, and is never extended. A book is in at most one cohort. Only a listed book may be screened or killed on that cohort's window, or cite it. A book declared later belongs to a later cohort. The hours of a cohort's window are disclosed beside every earlier window they overlap, and every cell priced on any window counts in N (item 3). The same list is the cohort of item 7 and of item 8 (k). Hunt23 wave 1 is the books of agents A, B and C whose DECLARE and DECLARE-AMEND-1 sha256 were notebooked before the first hunt23 price.
   - **(a) Window.** One exploration window per cohort, written in a notebook entry before any October price: a list of closed tape hours from 2026-10-19T08:05:20Z. It is never extended. (It plays the role R9(d)'s W_e plays for J3: every hour in it is in-sample for every listed book and never counts for any of them.)
   - **(b) Clock.** Each book's clock falls after the window's last hour and after the last exit of any trade priced in it.
   - **(c) It can only kill.** It removes an Aug/Sep survivor whose deciding-cell mean is at most 0 in any of its four sub-cells (flat and pressure, each under strand rules sc and total). It never adds, ranks or picks a cell. A cell designed on October alone takes the R9(d) route with its own declaration of at most 12 cells.
   - **(d) Replay.** Through `ArenaEngine` on the tape only, with the gap rules on, the pinned engine shas and the book's own `StackConfig`. R9(b)'s 360-s filter applies. No C1-NF reproduction (§3.7). No other book's ledger or decision log is opened.
   - **(e) No peek.** Never price a registered, unread book's rule, or a retune of it, on that book's counted hours. That is an interim peek (DEC-014 (c)). §2 already bars a retune on a book's own counted days.
   - **(f) Labels.** October figures are labelled in-sample and never cited. Those cells count in N (item 3), and J3's overlapping window is disclosed next to them.
   - **Arena only.** A book whose design touched these hours is an arena book only, forever (§3.3; R9(a)). Neither it, nor a variant of it, nor any book selected with its exploration registers as a push-family book on walk 2.
5. **No second frozen copy** (ruling item 5). A second frozen copy of a book is not allowed, whether to run beside the parent or to shorten its confirmation. The copy would be a new `bot_id` that repeats the parent's decisions. It either sits inside the screen window or duplicates the confirmation, and it adds to N. The confirmation of §2 step 4 runs on the same `bot_id` (item 2). A retuned rule is still a new book (§1.2, §2). That is a different rule, not a copy.
6. **Canary prerequisites** (ruling item 5). This amendment allows no live send (§4). A canary exists only under its own DEC (§2 step 3). Before one is proposed from a screen pass, all of these hold:
   - quant-proof's check of the read on its exact files (§2 step 1);
   - the owner's OK (§2 step 2);
   - a canary DEC with small limits (§2 step 3);
   - total positive ex-best-day (§2: a book whose total is not positive ex-best-day is not proposed for a canary);
   - the worst-in-slot mean and its CI90 lower bound, quoted in the proposal (R7). If no worst-in-slot figure exists, the read says "worst-in-slot not measured" and no canary is proposed from that book until it does (R7);
   - Amendment 2 items 5 and 6 (the sell builder and the mint fetch). The ruling lists them without a scope, so they apply to every book:
     - **Sell builder** (Amendment 2 item 5): a builder that sells and closes the base ATA in one transaction. For a book Amendment 2 covers, that is the condition itself. For a book it does not cover, the rent-charged leg is the paper comparator for the canary, and that meets item 5.
     - **Mint fetch** (Amendment 2 item 6: Fetch, Retries and the close-blocked class): the structure-only mint-account fetch of the book's traded mints runs before ANY canary proposal. For a book Amendment 2 does not cover (for example HV4 if it ends up rent-charged as built), it is report-only: the close-blocked count is quoted in the proposal and never decides, so nothing changes after the clock. A close-blocking mint strands a live canary whatever the rent.
     - **H5 is not exempt.** Amendment 2 does not cover the H5 books (its item 10), so for them the fetch is report-only and the rent-charged leg is their comparator, as above. Both are required before an H5 canary is proposed from an arena screen pass. No existing canary DEC is relied on to cover this hazard, and a canary already running under another DEC is not touched by this item.

   Beyond those:
   - **No confirmation first.** §2 step 4 gates stake above the canary's limits only. A small canary needs no confirmation.
   - **Shortest honest confirmation.** §2 step 4 as written: the same `bot_id`, at least 100 attempts on at least 5 further UTC dates, p at most 0.05/c. The 5 dates are the binding limit (5 days at 30 attempts a day). Nothing shortens it.
   - **In parallel.** The canary may run inside the confirmation window. Live fills must still match paper (§2 step 4), and a canary result never replaces the confirmation.

7. **Bank pass** (Version 2, item 4, arena side only). A book whose rule passed a pre-registered holdout-bank read (under DEC-014's block budget of 2026-10-07) may use its first arena read as its §2 step 4 confirmation, at p at most 0.05/c, on the conditions below.
   - **Eligibility, fixed before the arena read exists.** The shortcut is honest only if the first arena read is designated as a confirmation before its data exists. A book must not be picked after its arena read.
     - (a) The bank pass is recorded on main before the book's registration (§1.2). Only a book registered after it may use this item, and its registration record names the bank pass it relies on.
     - (b) The book's `frozen_sha` is the code the bank read ran, as pinned in the bank's Part 2, with md5 decision-equivalence as the ruling's item 4 requires.
     - A retune, or a book registered before its bank pass is recorded, is an ordinary arena book (items 1 and 2).
   - **How.** The first arena read of that `bot_id` is made as §2 and item 2 say: the same gate on its deciding cell, one read, at the outcome-blind instant. It is a confirmation test and adds 1 to c at that read, whatever its result (§2 step 4: failed and NOT_DECIDABLE ones are included); c at that read includes it. If it clears the gate and its p (§2 step 4's statistic, the larger p of the two fail models, as item 3 reports it) is at most 0.05/c, it is also the book's step 4 confirmation, and no further window is run for it. Live fills must still match paper (§2 step 4).
   - **Fallback, counted twice.** If it clears the gate with p above 0.05/c, it is an ordinary screen pass: item 2's confirmation runs on further dates, and that confirmation is a second test, so it adds 1 more to c. c counts confirmation tests, not books, so a book that takes two tests on disjoint data is counted twice, and each test is held to 0.05/c with the other in c. A bank pass does not leave a book worse off than a book with no bank pass: its fallback is the ordinary path. A FAIL or NOT_DECIDABLE at the first read is final for the `bot_id` (§2).
   - **Never canary evidence by itself.** A bank pass earns registration and an arena slot. It does not stand in for any of §2 steps 1 to 3 or for item 6's list. EXP-012 passed fresh-0903 and then failed forward.
   - **What this item is not.** It does not define the bank's design, open a block or claim one. DEC-014's block budget and the bank's own pre-registration (Part 1 and Part 2) do that. Hunt23 wave 1 is not bank-eligible (the manager's decision, recorded in the pinned ruling). For a later wave, a bank Part 1 merges before any price of that wave's cohort, the closed list of item 4 (item 8 (k)).
8. **What taints a later read** (ruling item 6, pinned). Each of these taints the read it touches. A read states every taint it touches in its multiplicity block (item 3). Where the ruling gives a consequence, it is given here.
   - (a) A screen or kill rule that decides on strand rule "total" only. `tools/arena_score.py` decides on four sub-cells (lines 33 to 35: flat and pressure, each under "sc" and "total").
   - (b) A cell that matches C1-NF's decision grid and exit, priced anyway (EXP-025 §4; §3.7 above). Books with a clock before 2026-10-24T02 trade inside the C1-NF seal, and a long-hold agent works in C1-NF's universe, so such a cell is dropped before pricing.
   - (c) The rent-0 universe (Amendment 2 item 6) is the same as EXP-024's V-range universe. A cell without R9(b)'s 360-s filter cannot run before 2026-11-14, and adding the filter after a screen makes it a new rule.
   - (d) Pricing a registered, unread book's rule, or a retune of it, on that book's counted hours (item 4 (e)).
   - (e) Siblings forward. If several near-identical books run, the best of them at the read is in-sample.
   - (f) A DECLARE sha256 that lives only in a `/data` file, which can change. It needs a notebook entry with its instant before the first price, or the whole screen counts as undeclared.
   - (g) Repeat screens on the same four blocks. Every priced cell counts, and each family adds to m.
   - (h) Wallet and creator history. A forward book can build it only from the tape (R6), so an August screen on full history does not carry over.
   - (i) Edits to shared engine files while books are running (R5(c)).
   - (j) Out-of-memory events or restarts cause feed gaps, which make every open position in that process NOT_DECIDABLE (R4, R5). A memory cap is set per process, and J3's 64 GB job is planned around.
   - (k) A bank Part 1 merged after any price of the cohort it serves (the closed list of item 4). (Version 2 numbers this 6(j), which collides with (j) above.)

Unchanged by this amendment: the promotion gate and its thresholds; §2's deciding cell, its one read per `bot_id`, its outcome-blind read instant and step 4's 100 attempts on 5 further dates at 0.05/c; Amendments 1 and 2 (the rent of Amendment 2, its scope, and the H5 books); every clock already registered; §3; the ledger's hours, owners and domain seals; DEC-014's block budget. The holdout bank's own design (the rest of the ruling's item 4) is not part of this amendment, and fresh-0802, fresh-0808 and fresh-0828 are not claimed or reassigned here. A screen pass is still a screen (§2), not a profit claim. No book has cleared the promotion gate.

### Amendment 4 (written 2026-10-21T18:41:30Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0; before the clock of any book it covers and before any arena read it touches; outcome-blind; status: DRAFT until merge)
DEC-031 Amendment 4: an automatic, narrower A3 rule for NEW H5 arena books (program-only redeploys), a replacement for Amendment 3 item 1's P0 drop rule, and the truncated-log rule for the H5 a2 reads. This turns quant-proof's rulings of 2026-10-21 into DEC text (items 1 to 3 below) and does not change the rulings' substance. No arena tape, ledger, `bots.json` line, outcome, fill, exit or P&L was opened to write it. In view: the two rulings, the owner's decision, the a1 attempt counts quoted in the a3 ruling (about 36 attempts in about 3 days, counts only) and the A3 daily records.
- Rulings, pinned in the repo, with the `/data` source that is mutable (the repo copy is the cited text):
  - `ARTIFACTS/lab/qp-ruling-1021-a3.md`, sha256 `286d21ae48aac13d5e3d093d845805aac5fc0c88df1fe779d36f0076be50c6e3`. That repo copy is the source file plus a 2-line provenance header; the source `/data/mal/hunt-1008/push-1016/hunt24/h5-canary/QP-RULING-1021-a3.md` is sha256 `e777b8966768c4562bd3a500b8b73f4f5cc0d1a1f8187131a5d81e23a85af8ed` and is unchanged below the header. It was written 2026-10-21T17:52:27Z, with an addendum of 17:54:19Z that carries Q6. Items here use its Q3, its Q4 (the halt-chaining lapse) and its Q6.
  - `ARTIFACTS/lab/qp-ruling-1021-repin.md` (posted as quant-proof's comment 6323686152 on PR #780), sha256 `454f8244846f844b6b0bfbefc5135cd0ee8e22df9d97618ce668cd64a2213fed`. - Also used: quant-proof's comment 6323323264 on PR #778 (review of #778 at d01bd50): the deadline anchor and its two costs, the 48 h dead-man (C-4), the ERROR fallback, the cadence recommendation and the amendment contents it asks for. Its code changes C-1 to C-5 are #778's, not this DEC's.
- Owner decision: MiScusi notebook entry `n_VXxa1CEWcslpaA` (about 2026-10-21T17:51Z): rare strategies are welcome; the lab works with pump redeploys; API cap. It is relayed by the a3 ruling's addendum and was not opened by the writer. It is the direction behind items 1 and 2. It does not authorise any manual PASS and does not reach a1 (item 1.1).
- Takes effect on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR, and it must merge before the clock of any book it covers (the merge instant is the committer time of the squash-merge commit on origin/main, as in Amendment 2 item 2). If it merges after a book's clock, that book runs under the A3 rule as registered and there is no switch after the clock.

1. **Automatic A3 redeploy rule** (a3 ruling Q3 R1 to R7, with the repin ruling's C3', Q3 order and Q4).
   1. **Scope.** H5 arena `bot_id`s registered after the merge, and only those. It never applies to `h5v1-boostfloor-a1` or `h5v2-boostclock-a1` (they end under the unchanged A3 rule: any A3 HALT flag ends the counted window at the flag's instant), never to EXP-024, and never to a DEC-024 canary (a canary still halts on `pins_changed` and `program_changed`). The books file (item 1.10) says which rule a book runs under.
   2. **Hard halts, unchanged.** These flags halt at the flag's instant, as now, with no check: `boost_disabled`, `boost_share_low`, `boost_last_slice_early`, `boost_budget_or_slices_changed`, a core rule unevaluated on two consecutive daily runs, and a `pins_changed` that includes ANY account pin (`bonding_fee_config`, `pumpswap_fee_config`, `pumpswap_global_config`) or the fees program deploy slot. `synthetic_share_high` stays as pinned (above 0.35 is a flag; the pin is not rounded either way).
   3. **What starts the check.** A `pins_changed` whose ONLY changed items are the pump and/or PumpSwap PROGRAM deploy slots starts an automatic upgrade check. It is a MiScusi scheduled or `after`-chained job on the host, never a session cron, and it runs on the cadence of item 1.8.
   4. **The check passes (exit 0) only if ALL hold:**
      - (a) the other five flags of the same run are clear and evaluated;
      - (b) the fee-config and global-config account sha256 values equal the pins;
      - (c) decoder parity: the frozen book's decode path on the pinned sample rule (the first N >= 500 pump and PumpSwap transactions, starting 150 slots after the redeploy's slot) shows 0 undecodable or unknown-discriminator events of the types the rule reads, prefix parity 0 inequalities against the pinned independent IDL decode, and the per-event trailing-byte set of item 1.7;
      - (d) the discriminator set and event layout lengths seen equal the pinned set;
      - (e) BOOST: InitBoost on at least the pinned minimum of post-deploy V-range pools, and budget and slice medians within +/-20% of the pins;
      - (f) the docs IDL for the instructions and events used is unchanged, or the fetch fails closed;
      - (g) the whole check finishes within 24 h (`CHECK_WINDOW_S`) of the earliest unresolved write's instant (item 1.6, Deadline);
      - (h) frozen guard: the git blobs of the decoder files the frozen book imports equal the blobs pinned from the book's frozen sha, else ERROR.
      As built (`tools/a3_redeploy_check.py`), (c) and (d) run on TWO samples, both starting 150 slots after the newest changed deploy slot: a LOG SAMPLE (the first 500 successful pump or PumpSwap transactions, `Program data` lines) and a MIGRATE SAMPLE (the first K = 40 settled migrate transactions, fetched whole, logs AND emit_cpi inner instructions; fewer than 20 usable is ERROR). The BOOST evidence of (e) comes ONLY from the migrate sample's post-deploy graduations (at least 5 non-mayhem WSOL graduations and 3 boost pools, else ERROR); the daily record's own boost numbers are not used for (e). The sample rule, N, K and the offset are constants in the pinned tool blob of item 1.11.
   5. **After a PASS: record only.** The window continues. Each such redeploy is disclosed at the read (slot, UTC, check job id), with the attempts decided between the deploy instant and the PASS (counts only), and the read states how many record-only redeploys its window held. The pins file is then updated by a records PR (quant-proof OK). Until then the daily run shows `pins_changed` again for the same deploy. The tool treats an already-PASSed deploy slot as covered (keyed by program and slot, `name:slot`, in the append-only state file, with the check job id recorded), so one deploy is never classified twice. If a later audit finds a decode change the check missed, the read is NOT_DECIDABLE. That no book traded on the new layout is not a reason to keep a window.
   6. **After a FAIL, an error, a timeout or no run: hard halt.** The window ends at the REDEPLOY instant, and that instant is the earliest UNREVIEWED program write (the "fail instant", defined in item 1.9), not the flagging run's instant. It is fixed in advance and no human judgment enters either branch. There is no manual override: nobody can turn a FAIL into a PASS, and a PASS cannot be vetoed after counts are seen. An ERROR (check job crashed or cannot decide) whose redeploy instant is not yet known halts at the check's RUN instant.
      - **Deadline.** The check must finish within 24 h of the earliest pending redeploy: the deadline is anchored at the earliest write that has no PASS yet, not at the flagging run (the a3 ruling's R3 said 24 h from the flagging run; this is stricter, and it binds only because this amendment states it). Source: quant-proof's comment 6323323264 on PR #778 (on `tools/a3_redeploy_check.py` at d01bd50: `deadline = deploy_ms + 24 h`), which asks that the amendment state this verbatim and disclose its two costs. The two costs, in QP's words:
        - "with one run a day, a redeploy that lands just after a run will time out and halt the book";
        - "one missed daily run times out every pending redeploy".
        Hence the cadence of item 1.8 (at least twice a day).
        Additional, also true and stated here: (a) a later redeploy that arrives while an earlier one is pending does not extend the deadline; (b) a FAIL of any pending write ends the window at the EARLIEST unreviewed write (item 1.7 and repin ruling Q4), and a pending write not yet checked counts as unreviewed.
      - **Dead-man.** The dead-man is `python -m tools.a3_halt deadman` (function `deadman`; constant `DEADMAN_S` = 48 h). Per new-rule book the anchor is the LATER of the newest applied decision's `run_utc` (from the append-only `a3-applied.jsonl`) and the book's own `registered_at_ms` (or `clock_start_ms`); with no applied log the anchor is the registration instant alone. A book whose anchor is more than 48 h old is ended at (anchor + 48 h). Silence is never a PASS. It runs on the fast-0 side, because research-0 cannot report its own absence, and it is the first step of `scripts/ops/a3-daily.sh` and runnable alone as `sh scripts/ops/a3-daily.sh deadman` (no RPC, no daily record). Limits, stated plainly: it has no anchor before the first applied decision other than the registration instant, and it only fires if the host-side schedule runs at all, so the `deadman` mode is scheduled as its own job at another hour. Source: quant-proof's comment 6323323264 on PR #778, item C-4.
      - **ERROR before the redeploy instant is known.** An ERROR raised before the redeploy instant is known halts at the check's RUN instant (`run_ms`), because no redeploy instant exists in that case. Source: the same comment 6323323264 on PR #778, section (1) ("acceptable only because no redeploy instant exists ... the amendment must say so"). An ERROR once the instant is known halts at the redeploy instant (item 1.6). `run_ms` applies only when no fail-closed bound is found, that is, no `deploy_utc` is pinned for the flagged program and no earlier daily record shows the pinned slot (`failclosed_bound_ms` raises, the check ERRORs at `run_ms`); whenever a program write is flagged and its enumeration fails, the bound of "Enumeration that fails" below applies (always <= `run_ms`).
      - **Enumeration that fails.** For a program whose write enumeration failed or did not reach the pinned slot, the halt instant is no later than the `run_utc` of the newest earlier daily record that still showed the pinned slot (else the pinned deploy's own instant), and a sticky non-PASS state entry for that program lowers it further; programs that did enumerate keep their earliest unreviewed write; the final instant is the minimum. A broken PREVIOUS daily record is treated as all-unevaluated and does not discard today's halt. Requirement (quant-proof B-1, comment 6325295176): every program entry of the monitor pins `tools/pump_structure_pins.json` carries `deploy_utc`; without it, when no earlier record shows the pinned slot, the code falls back to the run instant, which is not fail-closed. A pins records PR without `deploy_utc` on every program entry is refused. (Today pump, pumpswap and fees all carry it.)
      - **Stale or unreadable record.** If the newest daily line is older than 30 minutes (`MAX_RECORD_AGE_S`) or unreadable, `a3_redeploy_check` exits 11: no decision, nothing applied, no applied-log line (so the dead-man is not reset), and the job is loud. It does not halt by itself; the dead-man (48 h) is the backstop. This is the code's behaviour, stated as built. quant-proof (comment 6326240167) accepts it without a code change only with these three rules:
        - (a) no read of a covered book is taken unless an applied A3 decision (a line in `a3-applied.jsonl`) has `run_utc` at or after the read's boundary, and no write in the window is pending;
        - (b) a later applied decision may move a window end earlier (retroactive), never later (`halt_books` only moves an end earlier);
        - (c) a dead-man end is final for the `bot_id`, even if a later run finds no redeploy.
   7. **Pin design: enumerate writes, not a per-day hash** (repin ruling Q4, with C3'(b) and (c); source: quant-proof's comment 6323686152 on PR #780, which is the repin ruling's text).
      - The tool pages `getSignaturesForAddress(programdata)` back to the pinned slot. It pages at most 8 pages of 1000 (`MAX_WRITE_PAGES`); if the pinned slot is not reached, or a candidate transaction stays unreadable after retries (only a `null` answer is retried, 5 tries), it fails closed (ERROR, bounded as in item 1.6). A write is a successful transaction with a BPF-loader `Upgrade` instruction, top-level or inner (the real upgrades are Squads multisig CPIs). The pump enumeration was observed live (job #1312, public RPC: exactly two writes, slot 458888140 at 2026-10-19T15:46:16Z and slot 458921440 at 17:47:02Z). The PumpSwap enumeration was NOT observed live: it failed closed on public RPC (`http 413 on getTransaction`), and the 2026-10-19 15:46Z PumpSwap write is still not observed. Disclosed in item 1.14.
      - Every successful upgrade write is its own redeploy, with its own instant and its own item 1.4 check. Two writes on one UTC day are two redeploys.
      - The per-event trailing-byte layout is pinned in `tools/a3_redeploy_pins.json` (`trailing_bytes`): {TradeEvent, BuyEvent, SellEvent, WithdrawEvent: 8 bytes; every other pinned event: 0}. Each pinned event has exactly one allowed count (`[8]` or `[0]`); a looser pin (for example `[0, 8]`) is ERROR, and any other count seen in a sample is a HARD FAIL. The tool's tests cover the loose forms (`test_loose_trailing_pins_are_error`, `test_any_other_trailing_count_is_a_hard_fail`). The programdata sha256 values the set was derived from are stored as `reviewed_programdata`, a RECORD ONLY that no code compares against (a new redeploy has a different hash by definition and is judged by the checks, not by that hash).
      - **C3'(c).** A non-zero value in the added 8-byte field on a WSOL-quoted PumpSwap trade or a SOL-quoted bonding trade is a HARD FAIL of the check in both samples (so the halt instant is the redeploy instant of item 1.6, not the flag's), because its meaning would then be unknown inside the universe. A non-zero value on a non-SOL quote is allowed and counted; an unreadable pool counts as inside the universe. The sample evidence the ruling cites (0 non-zero on WSOL-only transactions; every non-zero pump TradeEvent carried a non-SOL `quote_mint`) came from the ruling's scratch split and is not relied on here. The field's meaning is a hypothesis (a quote-side transfer-fee amount) and is not needed.
      - A layout change outside this pinned set fails the check (item 1.4(d)), whatever the prefix parity.
   8. **Cadence and halt wiring.** The daily A3 job runs at least twice a day (>= 2 per 24 h), e.g. 07:10Z and 19:10Z, as ONE host-side MiScusi job on mal-fast-0 (`scripts/ops/a3-daily.sh`), plus the `deadman` mode as its own job at another hour. Session crons are never the guard (CLAUDE.md, lesson of 2026-10-15). The job order is: dead-man, the structure monitor, `tools.a3_redeploy_check`, then `tools.a3_halt apply`, which writes the window ends to the arena registry from the verdict file. The halt is NOT driven by the job's exit code: a halt that was applied prints `A3_HALT_APPLIED` and the script still exits 0; a nonzero exit means tool failure (2 books file or verdict refused with nothing written, OR `A3_HALT_PARTIAL`, where the halts that could be applied WERE written and an applicable rule had an empty list, with no applied-log line; 11 stale or unreadable record or unwritable state; 12 registry or applied-log write failed), needs a person, and is never a pass. (The a3 ruling Q4 records the lapse this closes: `halt` was not run on 2026-10-20 after notebook entry #1271 said "record only".) The job is not started against a live daily file before the monitor re-pin is merged and one official run on the new pins shows all six flags clear (item 1.12); the state file then starts empty. (The header comment of the merged `scripts/ops/a3-daily.sh` still says exit 2 means "nothing written"; the merged file stays as it is and a follow-up wording fix is owed. This DEC does not copy that wording.)
   9. **Every program upgrade write is paged back to the pinned slot (fail closed).** This is the enumeration of item 1.7 seen from the window: the "fail instant" of a window is the earliest upgrade write after the pin that has no PASS. For the 10-19 redeploy that means both the 15:46Z write and the 17:47Z write.
   10. **Books file and ids.** Coverage is an explicit books file, `$HOME/data/arena/a3-books.json`, exactly `{"legacy_bot_ids": [...], "new_rule_bot_ids": [...]}` (unique strings, none in both, every id registered, every registered `h5*` book in one of the lists, the books file is validated before any write, on every run; an applicable rule with an EMPTY list is caught later, in `plan()`: `apply()` first writes the other rule's halt, then exits 2 with `A3_HALT_PARTIAL` and no applied-log line, so one empty list never blocks the other rule's halt). Its sha256 and a verbatim copy go in a records PR before the clock of any covered book. A `bot_id` is listed under `new_rule_bot_ids` only if this amendment merges BEFORE its clock; a book registered before the merge is listed under `legacy_bot_ids` (or not at all if it is not an H5 book) and runs under the A3 rule as registered. (An earlier draft of this item said a `bots.json` field `a3_rule: "am4"`; no code reads such a field, so coverage is the books file.) Because `tools/arena_h5_score.py` is unchanged, `set_window_end` still only touches the prefixes `h5v1-boostfloor-` and `h5v2-boostclock-`; `tools/a3_halt.py` function `halt_books` applies the same window-end semantics to the explicit list, for any prefix, so a covered book of another prefix is reached (a3 ruling Q5, "Halt coverage"; tested in `tools/test_a3_halt.py`).
   11. **Pins, tools and scripts (as merged).** PR #778 merged as squash commit `22338cf88525b966aa055fbf7890a092f034dd2c` on origin/main (PR head `f0a25a5864f7bb5dc8fa38f19771f9e16b6d4cf6`; reviewer APPROVE 6326105057; quant-proof OK 6326086239 with M-2 6326107169). PR #780 (the monitor re-pin) is merged at `b1a4997`. Blob = `git rev-parse 22338cf:<path>`; sha256 of the file bytes. All of these are fixed before the clock of a covered book:
      - the check tool `tools/a3_redeploy_check.py`: blob `52cd901655d5600fc1633332a4cb3de2981abee9`, sha256 `922650653afca57969ad33da8436d323c00d884458e253d8d102e88d27d9cf82`. It holds the sample rule (500 txs, at most 300 slots scanned, offset 150), `MIGRATE_K` = 40, the 24 h window, the discriminator and layout checks and the WSOL/SOL split of C3'(c);
      - its tests `tools/test_a3_redeploy_check.py` (fixtures with a passing and a failing upgrade, an unknown discriminator, the trailing-byte set, the C3'(c) split; 111 tests at the merged head): blob `73b0243102a584bf96c589a4d0afb684a83eb3ac`, sha256 `1dd707caeaff41155593a6e399e52868883f83935412e1b431be7c8c0f25fea2`;

      - the check's pins `tools/a3_redeploy_pins.json` (IDL sha256 x3, trailing-byte set, ignored discriminators, `max_capped_share`, BOOST pins incl. `min_init_boost_in_migrate_sample`, frozen blobs, required migrate events, `reviewed_programdata` record): blob `6d70d9b8e8b37f4b29c733d7ad7a3a32ab382497`, sha256 `5fc93458bb95b7a1cfa40f76f84db3b96f4b444df6c22adfb01e5efe997d9ecc`;

The deploy-instant halt of item 1.6 is applied by `tools/a3_halt.py`, not by the scorer: `apply` -> `plan` -> `halt_books` ends the books of `new_rule_bot_ids` at the verdict's `halt_at_ms` (the redeploy instant, or the flag's instant when earlier) and the books of `legacy_bot_ids` at the flag's instant, with the scorer's registry lock, atomic write and "an earlier end is never moved later" semantics.    12. **Order of events before a covered a2 clock** (repin ruling Q3, copied, with status at the writing of this amendment).
