      - (e) **Memory.** Before the start, `MemAvailable` on research-0 and the sum of the running jobs' `MemoryMax` must leave room for the successor's request. The `MemoryMax` values come from the job list, never from the status view of #1561 or #1562. The successor's root and ledger filesystem (`/data2`) must have at least 25% free (the append floor of tools/arena_paper.py:297@9ed4c5f) and room for the 14-day window. A first refusal is a slip to the next 00:00Z, with the figures recorded. It is a stop only if the job is refused again for that later clock (Amendment 17 P3).
      - (f) **Daily jobs for the successor root, as HV4's:**
        - the three reviewer jobs at 00:05Z (CON-L, BIG-L, CON-P; none on the clock day, the first at 2026-11-10T00:05Z, through the window end);
        - the digests;
        - the 300 s window sampler, from clock - 15 min to END + 15 min, and its daily table (Amendment 6 items 2.1 and 2.6; a missing sample makes the hour FAIL);
        - the daily read-instant job from 2026-11-14T01:30Z;
        - the daily decision-log sha256 notebook entry (§1.2).
      - (g) **Spend.** The owner's 2026-10-28 line approves API spend on the Haiku trader; Helius credits are reported under CLAUDE.md "Credits".
        - API: the pinned caps of tools/hv4_arms.py apply to the successor's own root: 4.90 USD a day at cap (68.60 USD over the 14-day window; the run-out to END adds at most one more day's cap), and the 160 USD month guard.
        - Chain calls: the pinned caps of tools/hv4_chain.py.
        - The owner's 200 USD console limit stays the backstop.
        - The registration notebook entry states these figures.
      - (h) **No new smoke.** HV4's L5 real-model smoke PASS at `9ed4c5f` (QP-RULING-1022-hv4-am6 ADDENDUM 4) is the Amendment 6 item 1.3 evidence: the sha, model ids and sampling settings are the same. HV4's L3 unit tests (#1389) and L4 (no port) carry over the same way. A re-smoke at `9ed4c5f` would price the HV4 rule on HV4-counted hours, so it is not run.
   5. **The only stop reasons.** Each is notebooked with its instant before the stop. Nothing else stops the commitment.
      - A kill-review week, recorded on main before this amendment merges, that overlaps [registration, read]. None is recorded at writing.
      - A second memory refusal (item 1.4 (e)).
      - A quant-proof ruling of external impossibility: an item cannot be met for a reason outside the lab, such as the pinned model id no longer being served, a seal, or a proven host limit. An item not yet built, reviewed or merged is never such a reason.
      - The owner's word withdrawing the successors' API spend. This is the owner's authority. The stop record says which HV4 verdicts were in view.
   6. **What carries over: nothing.**
      - Each successor is a new `bot_id` with its own clock, P0 use, H, window and read. No HV4 attempt, trade, playbook version, manifest, ledger row or exclusion counts for it.
      - Its L and CON-P reviewers start from their own genesis and read only the successor's own paper outcomes.
      - A successor FAIL or NOT_DECIDABLE is final for that `bot_id`. A successor of an HV4 book that failed is a re-registration of the same rule as a new `bot_id`; it adds to N and discloses the earlier end (Amendment 4 item 2.3).
      - **No live carry-over.** A successor C0 needs that successor's own recorded screen PASS (DEC-032 §1 item 1), with the arms of item 2. An HV4 PASS, screen or confirmation, carries nothing to a successor at any rung. After an HV4 FAIL or NOT_DECIDABLE of the same arm, a successor C0 does not use the standing C0 owner line of DEC-032 Amendment 1 item 3. The owner is asked afresh, with that HV4 verdict stated in the question, and the item 4 file carries that fresh line (Amendment 17 item 4; QP-RULING-1027-s1 (c)).
      - A later text may add a live-rung carry-over only as a live rule. It may not add any condition that depends on an HV4 outcome or verdict to a successor's registration, sha, clock, H, window or read (the bar of Amendment 17 item 4).
   7. **N and disclosure.**
      - N + 9 at the successor clock (Amendment 4 item 2.5), counted as item 1.3 says. A successor screen pass enters confirmation automatically and adds to c (Amendment 3 item 2).
      - Every successor read prints:
        - the nine HV4 verdicts beside it (Amendment 3 item 8 (e));
        - item 1's disclosure sentence;
        - the day [2026-11-08T00Z, 2026-11-09T00Z) that neither window counts;
        - that the successor rule is the rule HV4 ran on HV4's window. An HV4 read and a successor read are two windows of one rule, and any later selection between them is in-sample.

2. **L-arm C0 eligibility.** This replaces the last two sentences of Amendment 15 item 5, and QP-RULING-1025-hv4-read-rule R2.2 (a) for the L arms.
   - **Playbook versions are part of the book.** A playbook version written by the book's own registered reviewer under §1.3 is not a code or prompt change for DEC-032 §1 item 4. That is a version written by the reviewer at the frozen code, instructions and model ids, with its manifest hashed, chained and logged before the first decision that uses it. §1.3 makes the whole system, versions included, the book, so the screen read of an L arm already read that system.
   - **Eligible arms.** After its own recorded PASS, an HV4 book may feed a C0 bridge if it is CON-F, CON-X, BIG-F, BIG-X, **CON-L or BIG-L**, under R2.2 (b) to (g), declared in the canary copy before the first send. CON-P and the R arms stay excluded: they are controls and not promotable (DESIGN §5). The same list applies to the successors of item 1, each on its own PASS. Amendment 15 item 5 then reads: "No process reads an HV4 decision row before that bot's read is recorded. After a recorded PASS, only CON-F, CON-X, BIG-F, BIG-X, CON-L or BIG-L may feed a C0 bridge, under QP-RULING-1025-hv4-read-rule R2.2 (b) to (g) as DEC-031 Amendment 21 items 2 and 3 read them, declared in the canary copy before the first send. CON-P and the R arms are not canaried."
   - **Per-row version check (L arms; fail closed).** Before an L-arm entry row becomes an order, the bridge checks all of these:
     - (i) the row's decision log chain (seq, prev, sha) is intact from the bot's first row;
     - (ii) the version in force is the one named by the newest `version` row before the entry in that log (tools/hv4_runner.py:1631@9ed4c5f), or the arm's genesis version (v0) if there is none. The `version` row's `effective_from_ms` is at or before the entry's decision instant, and no `version_refused` row follows it without a later `version_recovered`;
     - (iii) the manifest line it names (by `manifest_sha256`; the genesis line when (ii) found no `version` row) is in the arm's own manifest chain `<root>/manifests/<arm>.jsonl`, and every line from the genesis line up to it passes `check_manifest` (tools/hv4_review.py:850@9ed4c5f) against its predecessor. The genesis line has `prev_manifest_sha256` = 64 zeros and `written_ms` = the book's clock. Its `sha256` equals the genesis sha256 recorded before the clock (HV4: `ARTIFACTS/lab/hv4-registration-1023.md` row "Genesis (E3b)"; a successor: item 1.4 (c)). `check_manifest` is called with `frozen_sha`, `reviewer_sha256` and `v0_playbook_sha256` set. Every line has `commit` naming the frozen sha (one may be an abbreviation of the other, as `check_manifest` accepts; the code lane does not require literal 40-hex equality), `model` = the pinned model id (`claude-haiku-5-5`, as registered), and the `reviewer_sha256` that the frozen code computes for its reviewer instructions (`review_system_text`; tools/hv4_review.py file sha256 `8f0dfaada9e77a7e2d6d76c574f70ee10d0c7af7433c96f15da8c2de32cc7325`);
     - (iv) when there is a `version` row, its `playbook_sha256` equals the manifest line's.

     Failures:
     - (i) A break of the decision log's own chain is `chain_break` as built (DEC-032 §5 item 3): no row is trusted or read after it, so only the time-stop and stop-loss backstops close open positions (tools/arena_live_bridge.py:26-28).
     - A manifest line that (ii) names but that is not present on the bridge host (not yet carried) refuses that row, skipped with reason `version_unverified`. It latches nothing.
     - Any other failure of (ii) to (iv), on a line or chain that is present, latches `version_check`: a hard latch, cleared only by the manager with the bridge stopped. It refuses new buys only; book exit rows of open positions keep running, as under `feed_break`.
   - **R2.2 (c) for an L arm** reads: the bridge reads `decisions/<that bot_id>/` and that arm's own manifest chain file, read-only, and nothing else. It reads whole manifest lines (`check_manifest` hashes every field) and parses each line only to run the checks of (iii) and (iv). It logs or prints no field of a line other than `seq`, `version`, `written_ms`, `effective_from_ms` and the sha256 fields (never `knobs`, `vetoes`, `changes`, `rejected` or `shown_ids`). It opens no playbook text, bundle, audit, request or response file, and no card store, ledger, other arm or `bots.json`. The `version` rows of the decision log already carry knobs and vetoes, so no new seal class arises.
   - **R2.2 (b) for an L arm:** the API-returned model id of the trader calls, and the `model` of every manifest line, must be `claude-haiku-5-5` (or the same single id) for the whole window. Otherwise the arm is ineligible.
   - **No feedback from live.** A reviewer bundle holds only the arm's own paper trades, its skipped cards with their replayed paper outcomes, and its own playbook state; nothing from any other arm (tools/hv4_review.py module docstring, "WHAT A BUNDLE HOLDS", @9ed4c5f). No live fill, twin or parity figure reaches any reviewer bundle. Nothing in this item changes the paper book (R2.2 (f)).
   - **Code owed (not in this records PR).** Today `tools/hv4_live_c0.py` refuses `-L` (`REFUSED_ARM_SUFFIXES`, :225; `arm_refusal`, :359-366), `tools/hv4_gap_relay.py` calls the same check, and no per-row version check exists. A separate code PR, with its own QP OK and reviewer verdict:
     - allows exactly `hv4-CON-L` and `hv4-BIG-L` (and their `hv4s1-` successors), and keeps `-P` and `-R` refused;
     - implements the check above, with tests;
     - carries the manifest chain file to the bridge host with the decision rows.

     Until that code is merged and installed, the L arms stay refused in code.

3. **HV4 C0 feed latch.** For HV4 C0 copies and the successors' copies, this replaces DEC-032 §5 item 2 as built in tools/hv4_live_c0.py:819-821 and :876-901.
   - **Today.** Every gap kind of either stream latches `feed_break`: the book's tape gaps and the forwarder's transport events (`GAP_BREAK_KINDS`). The clear needs 10 minutes gap-free AND a root-owned review file naming exactly the newest gap instant. At the audited gap rates no person can keep that file current (audit 2.1 and H6).
   - **The rule it follows.** The replacement follows the rule that excludes a trade from HV4's paper read: §1.4, as implemented by `on_gap` (tools/arena_paper.py:920-931@9ed4c5f), the engine-silence and slot-jump gaps that `on_row` adds (:964-970), and `_gap_hit` (:1221-1228), which also matches a `pumpfun` gap to a `pump_bonding` trade.
   - **Consistent with DEC-032 Amendment 5 (DRAFT).** That draft (lane q50-c0-feed, code branch `claude/q50-c0-gap-hold`) covers H5 executor canaries and leaves the HV4 bridge path out of its scope. This item applies its rule to the HV4 streams, with the one difference stated in item 3.3.

   1. **Coverage-losing gap: short hold and per-entry refusal.** A coverage-losing record is any record that `tools/hv4_gap_relay.py` carries (`RELAY_KINDS`, :31):
      - a tape `gap` row with `data_loss` true;
      - a tape `gap` row of kind `process_restart`, `feed_restart` or `feed_start`;
      - a feed `start` row;
      - a silence above `gap_silence_ms` (60,000 ms) between consecutive trade rows;
      - a `takeover`. The relay carries it whether or not it lost data; that is stricter than the paper rule and kept (fail closed).

      On such a record:
      - **Short hold.** No new buy for **60 s** after the record reaches the bridge. Each new record restarts the 60 s. Config may lengthen the hold up to 600,000 ms and never shorten it.
      - **Per-entry (per-pool) refusal.** No buy is ever sent on an entry row whose decision instant is at or before the instant of a coverage-losing record of its program that the bridge has seen. The paper read excludes that trade (the gap lies in its decision-to-exit span), so the live trip could never be compared. The skip is logged `feed_gap`.
      - **Open trips.** Every live trip whose exit has not landed is marked `gap`. It is excluded from parity statistics by the paper read's rule (§1.4) and counted, as DEC-032 §5 item 2 says.

      A gap row of a program the book does not trade excludes none of its paper trades: `_gap_hit` matches only the trade's own program, `all` or none. The relay as built carries kind and instant only. Until it carries the program field, every relayed record holds (fail closed).
   2. **Redundant reconnect: no hold.**
      - **Tape.** An arena tape `socket_reconnect` row with `data_loss` literally false is not data loss: some socket of that program stayed subscribed at every instant (tools/arena_feed.py:600-606@9ed4c5f). The paper engine only counts it (`gap_soft`), and the relay does not carry it. It holds nothing and marks nothing.
      - **Transport.** The forwarder's transport events are not feed gaps of the book: `stall`, `resume`, `disconnect`, `reconnect`, `error`, a `mismatch` and a trimmed local torn tail. They move decision rows and lose no print. The puller resumes at the exact byte offset and checks every row against the decision log's chain rule; a chain or offset mismatch stops it and appends nothing (tools/decision_forwarder.py, module docstring). These events leave §5 item 2's latch.
      - **The guards that stay.** `transport_stall` refuses new buys while the forwarder is not live (tools/arena_live_bridge.py:701-702). A decision row older than 5,000 ms is refused (DEC-032:68; tools/arena_live_bridge.py:65). The bridge's `chain_break` latch on any decision-log chain fault is DEC-032 §5 item 3 (hard), not a gap rule.
   3. **Review within 24 h; an unreviewed gap latches.**
      - Every gap record of both streams is ledgered with its kind and instant and listed in the daily report.
      - The manager reviews them at least once a day. The review is recorded in the root-owned file of `GAP_CLEAR_DIR` (`{"bot_id", "reviewed_gap_ms"}`), where `reviewed_gap_ms` is the newest coverage-losing instant reviewed. The manager writes that file (root via sudo on the bridge host, mal-fast-0). It needs no Helm step. One daily write covers every gap up to `reviewed_gap_ms`.
      - A coverage-losing record more than 24 h old, with an instant later than `reviewed_gap_ms`, latches `feed_break` (hard, new buys only; exits continue) until a review file covers it.
      - No human acknowledgement is needed before buys resume after a gap.
      - This is stricter than DEC-032 Amendment 5 (DRAFT) item 4, by the 24 h latch: an unattended canary on a degrading feed stops within a day.
   4. **P7 at C0** (as DEC-032 Amendment 5 (DRAFT) item 6b).
      - P7's `gap`-share part (above 5%, or 2 `gap` trips before 20 trips, DEC-032 Amendment 1 item 1) makes parity NOT_DECIDABLE and blocks any step-up. It does not create `STOP`.
      - Its `no_twin` part, and its missing, unreadable or stale state part, stay soft halts.
      - Money is unchanged: a `gap` trip stays inside every C0 money limit (stake, open cap, daily and total stop, wallet floor, committed loss). It is only excluded from parity.
      - Why it matters for HV4: HV4 trips can stay open for hours. Under the current rule almost every trip on a gappy tape would be `gap`, and two `gap` trips would soft-halt the canary.
   5. **Residual, declared.** The engine's slot-jump rule (`gap_slots` 150) is not relayed (tools/hv4_gap_relay.py docstring). The bridge does not mark `gap` a live trip whose span holds such a jump. Not changed here.
   6. **Code owed (not in this records PR).** A separate code PR in tools/hv4_live_c0.py (and the parity tool for P7), with tests and its own QP OK and reviewer verdict:
      - `GAP_FREE_MS` 600,000 becomes a 60,000 ms hold, with a 60,000 ms floor checked at start (`guard_problem`);
      - the transport kinds leave `GAP_BREAK_KINDS`;
      - the per-entry refusal;
      - the 24 h review latch, in place of `gap_clear_problem`'s per-gap equality;
      - the P7 change of item 3.4;
      - a stated bound on the relay-to-bridge latency of a coverage-losing record (QP N3);
      - an md5 decision-equivalence replay wherever the decision path is touched (CLAUDE.md runner rule).

      Until it is installed, §5 item 2 as built applies.

4. **HV4 owner line: a root-owned file, no sha pinned in code** (audit rank 4). For HV4 C0 copies and the successors' copies, this replaces the gate of tools/hv4_live_c0.py:464-465.
   - **The file.** The owner's C0 confirmation for an HV4 `bot_id` is a root-owned file that Helm writes on the owner's own word: the owner's standing line of DEC-032 Amendment 1 item 3, or a fresh line.
     - One regular file per `bot_id` under `/etc/mal-hv4-c0/` (the path is a code constant).
     - root:root, not group- or other-writable, link count 1.
     - It holds, on a line of its own, exactly `OWNER_CANARY_CONFIRMED: bot_id=<bot_id>` (DEC-032 Amendment 1 item 3 "Use").
     - Helm reports its sha256 and the instant it was written; the manager notebooks them.
   - **The gate.** The bridge refuses a live buy unless all of these hold:
     - the file is present and exact;
     - `LIVE_OK` names the `bot_id` (root-owned, re-read before every buy);
     - `TIER` reads T1;
     - the bot's recorded read is PASS (`read_record`).

     At the first good check the bridge pins the file's (st_dev, st_ino, sha256) in its state, as QP N9 does for the read record. A later rewrite, replacement or removal latches `owner_line` (hard, new buys only; exits continue) until the manager clears it with the bridge stopped.
   - **No sha256 of the owner file or of a DEC copy is compiled into code.** `CANARY_DEC_SHA256` (:465), and the reinstall it forces after the owner's yes, go away in the code lane's PR. The merged DEC copy still quotes the owner's line verbatim (DEC-032 §2). The copy's records after the first send cite Helm's sha256 and instant.
   - **Why this is safe.** The owner's word still gates the send twice: this file and `LIVE_OK`, both written only by Helm, as root, on the owner's word. The DEC copy still merges with QP OK and a reviewer verdict before any send. Only the code PR and reinstall after the owner's yes are removed; Helm's last two turnarounds were about 21 h and 22 h (audit 2.3).
   - **Code owed (not in this records PR).** The change in tools/hv4_live_c0.py, with tests, under its own QP OK and reviewer verdict. `LIVE_REFUSAL` (:455) stays until every precondition PR of the HV4 copy is installed.

5. **Why this is outcome-blind.**
   - **When written.** At 2026-10-30T15:39:03Z, revised at 15:42:41Z and at 15:56:51Z (`date -u` on mal-research-0), all before any HV4 read verdict exists. The first HV4 reads, #1829 (`hv4-BIG-X`) and #1830 (`hv4-CON-X`), start at 2026-11-01T01:35Z.
   - **Item 1** registers all nine successors whatever the verdicts, at a sha, clock, H, window and read rule fixed here. A successor's window starts one day after HV4's ends, and nothing carries over. It cannot select on an HV4 outcome, because none exists, and no later outcome can change what it commits to.
   - **Item 2** fixes, before any verdict, which arms may be canaried after their own PASS. It changes no read, trade set or verdict. Written after an L verdict, it would be selected on that verdict. That is why it lapses if this amendment merges late.
   - **Items 3 and 4** act only on the live C0 path of a book with a recorded PASS. They change no paper read, exclusion, trade set or verdict, and use no outcome.
   - **In view when writing.** `hv4-BIG-X` and `hv4-CON-X` have a written read instant B = 2026-10-30T00:00Z, and the seven other HV4 books had none at that boundary. This comes from the manager's state log and the audit, from the pinned instant tool's decision-timestamp counts; no count itself was seen. It cannot move any item: item 1 covers all nine, item 2 is fixed before any L read, and items 3 and 4 touch no read.
6. **Unchanged:**
   - the promotion gate and every threshold (n >= 100; >= 5 UTC dates with a majority positive; CI90 lower bound > 0, 1,000 draws, seed 1, 5th percentile; ex-top-3 > 0; flat 15% and pressure-fail at slope scale 1);
   - §2's one read per `bot_id`, its deciding cell and its outcome-blind instant;
   - Amendment 15 for the nine HV4 books: their sha, clock, H, END, read instant, trade set, caps, confirmation and worst-in-slot step;
   - R2.2 (b) to (g), except as items 2 and 3 say;
   - DEC-032's limits, stops, wallet floor, P1 to P6, §5 items 1 and 3 to 7, §6 and §7;
   - the ban on a live send under this DEC (§4), and every seal.

   No HV4 job is touched.
7. **Disclosure.**
   - **In view:**
     - DEC-031 through Amendment 20, and DEC-032 through Amendment 4 and Amendment 2 Erratum 1, at origin/main `c92f23e3` (Amendment 18 Erratum 1, merged later as #1099, came in with a merge of main and touches nothing here);
     - the audit; QP-RULING-1025-hv4-read-rule (sha256 `c2343c9c6ef96256419c4e7f4eec1b83584163bfd3a1aff97ca726cecdc50955`); `docs/rules/hv4.md`; `ARTIFACTS/lab/hv4-registration-1023.md`;
     - the DEC-032 Amendment 5 DRAFT text (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/reports/2026-10-30/q50-c0-feed-DEC032-Am5-DRAFT.md`) and its code branch;
     - quant-proof's comment 6527127124 and the reviewer's comment 6527117571 on PR #1101; the `pgrep` guard lines of `t0-1025/E4-runner-seg1.sh` and `-seg2.sh` (scripts only);
     - code at `9ed4c5ff` and at main: `tools/arena_score.py`, `tools/hv4_live_c0.py`, `tools/hv4_gap_relay.py`, `tools/decision_forwarder.py`, `tools/arena_live_bridge.py`, `tools/arena_paper.py`, `tools/arena_feed.py`, `tools/hv4_runner.py`, `tools/hv4_review.py`, `tools/hv4_cards.py`.
   - **Not opened:** any HV4 ledger, decision row, card, manifest, playbook, bundle, digest, `bots.json`, score, preview, count, read-instant record, or job status or log (#1561, #1562, #1591, #1829, #1830); the arena tape; any Q50 or DBC output. No job was run.
   - **Not measured by this amendment:**
     - the arena tape's coverage-losing gap rate, which sets what item 3's hold costs HV4 in buy time;
     - the successors' memory, API and chain-call figures;
     - any HV4 count or outcome.

### Amendment 22 (DRAFT; number = the next free DEC-031 amendment at merge: Amendment 16 is held by open PR #1019, and Amendment 21 (PR #1101, main `72066560`) and Amendment 18 Erratum 1 (PR #1099, main `d8b6d35b`) are on main; a renumber at merge is a fill; written 2026-10-30T15:56:08Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0; records only; text from QP-RULING-1030-power-n Appendices A and B, adapted to `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1` only, with two records items added by the manager (items 9 and 10) and the feed item 11; before any qualifying read instant or read verdict of `h5v1q50-boostfloor-a1`, before the registration and clock of `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1`, and before any outcome of that book exists; outcome-blind; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict)
DEC-031 Amendment 22 (the power-n amendment): `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1` declares a larger screen size and a larger confirmation size, each with a calendar end, and its book end moves to 2026-12-01T00:00:00Z, S\*'s `SUCC_END_MS`. It also gives s1 its own arena root, registry, books file and A3 loop, a Helius websocket as its second feed provider, and a pre-clock feed-coverage test. Ruling: QP-RULING-1030-power-n (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/manager18/QP-RULING-1030-power-n.md`, sha256 `c86f0613e79d47e18f1bbb23a52b56ad20f4fc4a68e9e4babfe679e58d113631`, written 2026-10-30T15:40:56Z), rulings R1, R2 and R4 (a). Scope: `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1` only; `h5v1q50-boostfloor-a1` is unchanged (item 8.3) and `h5v1q60-boostfloor-a1` is withdrawn (item 8.2). The promotion gate and its thresholds do not change. No book has cleared the promotion gate. Q50 and s1 have 0 read out-of-sample trades. Nothing here is a profit claim.

- **Outcome-blind decision instant.** The numbers of items 2 and 8.1 were pinned in MiScusi notebook entry `n_F60AKfUlWM41kw` (kind decision, "PIN before Q50 read", session s_y1y4vFh5TCjy8A), written at **2026-10-30T15:44:00.787Z** (the notebook's server `createdAt` 1793375040787; its text's `date -u` stamp is 2026-10-30T15:43:47Z), so before 2026-10-31T00:20:00Z, the first score run that can write a qualifying `q50-read-instant.json`. The entry says it was written before any Q50 read verdict existed and with no Q50, s1, HV4 or other sealed outcome, count or verdict seen; Q50's 2026-10-30 boundary not qualifying was known only from the absence of the write. The merged numbers equal the pinned ones: N_s 200, S_cap clock + 10 d with at least 100, N_c 300, E_c and E 1796083200000. quant-proof read the full entry (2,424 characters, body sha256 `c8088ae33357422272cb5832794e73c19fa36eb6c67d0ee96d4de580842df880`) and found every number equal (QP comment 6527661211).
- **Takes effect** on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR (merge instant: the committer time of the merge commit on origin/main, as in Amendment 2 item 2). It must merge before 2026-11-04T00:00:00Z, when s1's registration window opens (Amendment 18 item 4; ruling section 6 step 2). If it has not merged by then, s1 registers under Amendments 17 and 18 as written and this amendment lapses for s1.

1. **Floor, not a cap (R1).** In §2 "When", in §2 step 4, in Amendment 11 items 1 and 3, and in every text that applies them, "at least 100 attempts (counted trades) on at least 5 (further) UTC dates" is a minimum. Amendment 3 item 6 ("Nothing shortens it") stands; this amendment only lengthens a read. This amendment uses that reading for `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1` only. It declares no size for any other book; another book needs its own amendment under R1's conditions C1 to C5.
2. **Declared size (Appendix A item 2).** s1 declares a screen size N_s with a screen cap S_cap and a book end E, and a confirmation size N_c with a confirmation end E_c (values in item 8.1).
   - **Screen instant.** The first 00:00Z boundary B with clock_start_ms < B <= E such that the attempts decided in [clock_start_ms, B) include either:
     - (i) at least N_s counted trades (Amendment 11 item 1) on at least 5 distinct UTC decision dates; or
     - (ii) B >= S_cap and at least 100 counted trades on at least 5 distinct UTC decision dates.

     B qualifies only once every attempt decided before B has closed or been excluded (§2). If there is none, the book ENDS NOT_DECIDABLE at E, final, counts only.
   - **Confirmation instant.** The first 00:00Z boundary B with T_s < B <= E_c (T_s is the screen read's instant) such that the attempts decided in [T_s, B) include either:
     - (i) at least N_c counted trades on at least 5 further UTC dates; or
     - (ii) B = E_c and at least 100 counted trades on at least 5 further UTC dates.

     B qualifies only once all of those attempts have closed or been excluded. If there is none, the confirmation is NOT_DECIDABLE at E_c, final for the `bot_id`.
   - **The count.** It uses only decision timestamps and the scorer's counted / excluded / open / refused class, as Amendment 11 item 1 does. It uses and prints no fill, exit, net, leg or P&L field.
3. **When it was declared (R1 C1).** s1 is not registered, so its screen size is declared before its registration and clock, as C1 requires; its confirmation size is declared in the same text. Both are declared before the first read verdict of any member of the H5-BOOSTFLOOR family (Q50, s1). The declaration is the notebook entry above, then this text, merged with QP OK and a reviewer verdict. It is never changed afterwards, never shortened and never extended.

9. **s1's own arena root, registry, books file and A3 loop (the manager's records item (a); replaces Amendment 18 items 6.1, 6.2, 6.4, 6.5 and 6.7 for s1).**
   - **Why.** `tools/a3_halt.validate_books` refuses the run (exit 2, no halt applied to ANY book) when any registered `h5*` bot in the registry is in neither A3 list (`tools/a3_halt.py:132` at origin/main `c92f23e3`; :121 at Q50's guard pin `0ec7fae8`). With one shared `bots.json`, s1 would have to be listed in loop 1's books file (Amendment 18 item 6.1's books v3), which ties s1 to Q50's guard pin and gives it neither the bounded write enumeration nor the fees-program check (#894, `666502a3`). If s1 were registered in Q50's `bots.json` and left off loop 1's books file, loop 1 would refuse every run and Q50's 48 h dead-man (Amendment 4) would end Q50. A second loop on the shared registry would have to list Q50, which changes Q50's guard after its clock (Amendment 4 preamble). A separate root avoids all three.
   - **The root.** s1 runs in its own arena root on mal-fast-0 (the `Q50S1_ARENA` path that `scripts/ops/q50s1-register.sh` and `scripts/ops/q50s1-shadow-start.sh` already take), with its own `bots.json` (only s1's line), its own A3 books file `{"legacy_bot_ids": [], "new_rule_bot_ids": ["h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1"], "fees_check_bot_ids": ["h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1"]}`, its own ledger, and its own A3 output directory (`A3_OUT`: daily record, state file, verdicts and applied log). The root path, the books file's sha256 and a verbatim copy go in the registration notebook entry and the records PR.
   - **Loop 2.** s1's A3 loop runs `scripts/ops/a3-daily.sh` at a new guard pin: a tag at a main commit that holds #894's bounded write enumeration and fees-program check, named with its commit in the registration record, with QP OK on that pin before registration. It runs with `A3_ENUM_ENV_FILE=/var/lib/mal/fast-listener/helius.env` (the bounded upgrade-write enumeration of Amendment 13 and its Erratum 1; calls recorded per run, credits reported in the daily note), on s1's books file only. **Guard (h) (QP comment 6527661211, ruling 3): loop 2 pins S\*'s own decoder blobs** (QP-RULING-1024-successor R7(b).3's preferred form; loop 1 is not touched). The pin is a tag at a main commit that holds #894 (`666502a3` is on main) plus exactly two changed paths: `tools/h5_shadow.py` = S\*'s blob `7f859b12deef87573a7314e49e125b5121eaa645`, and `tools/a3_redeploy_pins.json` with `frozen_sha` `5c2ab7c64b21358619e37068c6a53e4d183512ea` and `frozen_blobs` h5_shadow `7f859b12`, the other five blobs unchanged (`026a9cd9`, `238942a6`, `64d5e497`, `42daf5ad`, `1ca0a88c`, S\*'s and main's). A plain main tag would ERROR guard (h): main's `tools/h5_shadow.py` is F50's blob `f23cf492` while `tools/a3_redeploy_pins.json` names `1f7a89c`'s `23b9ccc2` (`tools/test_a3_redeploy_check.py::test_frozen_guard_matches_the_shipped_pins_at_head` fails at main for this reason), so the pre-clock green run would fail and s1 would end NOT_DECIDABLE before its clock. Never loop 1's pin.

A halt in [registration, clock) ends s1 NOT_DECIDABLE before its clock, final, N + 1. Loop 2's state file is new, so Amendment 18 item 6.2's sticky non-PASS check on Q50's state file does not apply.

10. **s1's second feed provider: a Helius websocket (the manager's records item (b)).**
    - **Why.** `wss://public.rpc.solanavibestation.com` refuses websockets with HTTP 429 (job #1842), so s1's two providers would be one (`wss://api.mainnet-beta.solana.com`), on the same IP as Q50's shadow.
    - **The provider.** s1's shadow, and its Amendment 6 smoke, use `wss://api.mainnet-beta.solana.com` and a Helius websocket (`wss://mainnet.helius-rpc.com/`, keyed). Helius stays the **second** `--ws-url` (QP ruling 4 (iv)): with `--sockets 3 --syn-sockets 1 --ws-url WS1 --ws-url WS2`, only PumpSwap socket 1 is on Helius and the pump-bonding feed stays on WS1 (S\* `build_pump_source` takes `urls[0]` at one syn socket, :3091).
    - **Rule: the key is never in any process command line (argv) or any unit description.** The start script reads it from `/var/lib/mal/fast-listener/helius.env` (variable `HELIUS_API_KEY`; ACL `user:claude:r` per Amendment 13 Erratum 1) into the process environment (an exported variable, never `env VAR=value` on a command line) or passes it on stdin, and the `--ws-url` value is built inside the shadow's Python process from there. S\*'s `tools/h5_shadow.py` takes the URL only as `--ws-url`, so the launch is a small launcher outside S\* that sets the argument list inside the process and runs `tools.h5_shadow` unchanged; it is reviewed with QP OK and a reviewer verdict. If no such launch is ready, the second provider is not Helius: the key is never put in argv. `systemd-run` gets a fixed `--description`. The key also never appears in the MiScusi job command or job log, the systemd journal or the start record (S\* strips the query from the logged argv, `redact_argv` at :2491, and from the start record's `endpoints`, :3289). /proc on these hosts has no `hidepid`, so any argv is readable by every local uid (QP comment 6527661211, C3).
    - **Checks (counts only).** Before the clock, the string `api-key` is counted in the smoke's log, the systemd journal, the start job's MiScusi job log, the start record and the shadow's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`: every count must be 0. mal-fast-0's `findmnt -no OPTIONS /proc` is recorded in the registration record.
    - **Ops line.** While s1's smoke or shadow runs on mal-fast-0, no command, agent, report or watchdog prints full process argv there: use `pgrep -f` for the pid only, `ps -o pid,rss,comm`, or `systemctl show -p MainPID,ActiveState`.
    - **Scope.** This extends the use of the shared key that Amendment 13 Erratum 1 set (A3 enumeration only) to s1's shadow feed and loop 2's enumeration. Manager decision, 2026-10-30, owner informed; the registration record cites the owner-informed message by id and instant (QP ruling 4 (ii)). QP finds no validity objection (ruling 4). Credits (ruling 4 (iii)): the at least 6 h smoke records the Helius credits it used, and the registration record projects them to 2026-12-01T00Z against the plan plus autoscale, because a run-out would both gap the feed and ERROR loop 2's enumeration, and an enumeration error halts s1 (Amendment 13 item 1, Amendment 4 item 1.6). The credits used are also recorded per day in the daily note (CLAUDE.md "Credits"). Q50 feed load (disclosed in the registration record, never a reason to change Q50): s1's smoke and shadow add api.mainnet-beta sockets on Q50's IP inside Q50's window. The endpoint list is a registration-record value (`endpoints` in the `bots.json` line, key-free); S\* is unchanged, so this is not a code change of the book.
    - **Code owed (`scripts/ops/q50s1-shadow-start.sh`; PR #1086 merged at main `bccfac48` without these changes, so the s1-scripts lane (branch `claude/s1-job-scripts`) makes them in its own PR; not edited here).** `WS2` is built at start from the env file into the environment (refuse to start if the variable is empty) and reaches the shadow by the launcher above, never argv; the check mode prints `WS2` without its query; `systemd-run` gets `--description=q50s1-shadow`; the header's "0 Helius credits" is corrected. Reviewed with QP OK and a reviewer verdict before the smoke.
11. **Pre-clock feed coverage (replaces any "at most 2 flagged gaps per hour" form; Amendment 18 item 8's smoke is lengthened for s1).** The Amendment 6 smoke (at S\*, with s1's own feed arguments of item 10, in a scratch root, for at least 6 h) PASSes this item only if the flagged-gap exposure of its own output is at most 2.5%: E_gap = |union of [t_ms - 340 s, t_ms]| / (the smoke's full hours), the union taken over every record with `"type": "gap"` whose `flags_pools` is not literally `false`. Only gap rows are read (fields `t_ms`, `kind`, `flags_pools`); no trigger, pool, strip or outcome record is opened (EXP-024 successor note part A). The per-hour count by kind is reported and never decides. Above 2.5%: no clock until a smoke on a changed feed passes; a feed change that keeps S\* is a registration-record change (endpoints); a code change is a new `bot_id`. This item never touches a running book. **E_gap is a lower bound on the excluded share** (QP comment 6527661211, C2). A flagged gap at instant g flags every pool the shadow follows at g (S\* `tools/h5_shadow.py:1330-1338`; a pool is followed from its first print to s0 + 400 s, `POOL_LIFE_S` at :236), and the scorer excludes a trade whose pool was flagged at any point from its first print to the end of the trade (`tools/arena_h5_score.py:436`, `gap_in_span`). So one flagged gap excludes the trades of pools with s0 in about [g - 400 s, g], and up to about 630 s for a trigger late in its 300 s window; the 340 s window above understates that. At about 0.27 flagged gaps an hour (E_gap about 2.5%) the excluded share is about 3.0% (400 s) to 4.7% (640 s), under the 5% cap (§1.4), which must also absorb every other exclusion route; at 2 an hour it is about 20% to 30%, far above the cap. A 2 h smoke with one isolated gap already gives E_gap 4.7%; over 6 h the item passes at most one isolated flagged gap. The 2.5% bar is an ops screen, not a validity rule. Arithmetic from the q50-read-pack lane report (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/reports/2026-10-30/q50-read-pack.md` section 4 (a)) as corrected by QP; not measured on any book's output.

### Amendment 19, follow-up: Q60 withdrawn (written 2026-10-30T15:56:08Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0; records only; before any registration, P0, `bots.json` line, tag or clock of the book it covers; outcome-blind; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict)
**`h5v1q60-boostfloor-a1` is WITHDRAWN: withdrawn before registration, 2026-10-30, notebook n_F60AKfUlWM41kw; no bot_id, P0 or clock.** The manager chose to withdraw under QP-RULING-1030-power-n R4 (a) (sha256 `c86f0613e79d47e18f1bbb23a52b56ad20f4fc4a68e9e4babfe679e58d113631`), before 2026-10-31T00:20:00Z and before any Q50 read verdict. The choice depends on no Q50 or s1 outcome, verdict, P&L, fill or count (Amendment 19's own bar, DEC-031:664); QP's reasons were structural: Q60's triggers nest Q50's on the same pools with the same exit and it is the worst discovery cell; it adds no power to s1's read; it would add 1 to N and widen the family line; its proof chain had two open STOPs; it needed a third H5 shadow on mal-fast-0.
- No `bots.json` line, P0 job, fixed-first P0 entry, tag `q60-base-1` or clock exists for it, so N does not change. Amendment 19 has no further effect; its memory erratum (QP-RULING-1030-s1-memory section 1.2) was not entered (Amendment 18 Erratum 1, last paragraph); Amendment 22 item 8.2 does not apply.
- The family line of Amendment 19 item 7 (three looks, 1 - 0.95^3 = 14.3%) is not printed. Every s1 read and every owner ask for a C0 canary of Q50 or s1 prints Amendment 18 item 9's two-look statement (1 - 0.95^2 = 9.75%) instead.
- `EXP/EXP-024-RULE-Q60.md` stays unchanged on main as the record of the withdrawn rule (its sha256 `f36afaae00edd5b8292fdbe732c6034251cc1371afb6c1e54b50e548a91717b9` is pinned in Amendment 19 item 1 and in the never-tagged Q60 code). The library row `l3-boost-h5q50-1` records the withdrawal of its variant B. Q60's PRs #1088, #1089 and #1093 and the never-merge PR #1083 are closed (checked 2026-10-30).
- A later Q\* = 60 book would be a new `bot_id` with its own amendment, decided before any outcome that could inform it, and would carry this withdrawal in its disclosure.

### Amendment 22, Erratum 1 (text verbatim from quant-proof's comment 6533921343 on PR #1121, 2026-10-30T21:33:36Z; records only; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict)
DEC-031 Amendment 22, Erratum 1 (records only; before the registration and clock of `h5v1q50-boostfloor-s1` and before any outcome of that book exists; outcome-blind; no condition depends on any Q50 or s1 outcome, count or verdict; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict). ### Amendment 23 (DRAFT; number = the next free DEC-031 amendment at merge: Amendment 16 is held by open PR #1019, Amendments 17 to 22 and their errata are on main; a renumber at merge is a fill; written 2026-11-01T03:09Z, revised 2026-11-01T03:32Z, from `date -u` on mal-research-0, on quant-proof's items C1 to C8 (comment 6552888187; ruling QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader) and the reviewer's items B1 to B5 (comment 6552790468); records only; before the first tip read, the clock, P0 count and `bots.json` line of any book it covers; outcome-blind; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict)
DEC-031 Amendment 23: arena books whose input is the fast-0 getBlock tip follower's tape (through the arena tip adapter, PR #1165, main `ca9c2822`, and a pinned writer) instead of `/data/mal/arena/tape`. Records only. It writes the rules that QP-RULING-1101-hv4-reads §4.1 (B) item 5, the QP verdict on #1165 (rounds 1 and 2) and QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader require. **Item 7 CHANGES §3 item 6's last clause for covered books; it does not clarify it.** No tape, tip file, mirror, ledger, `bots.json` line, decision row, outcome, fill, exit or P&L was opened to write it. No fast-0 file was read (no route from the writing host); every follower figure below is quoted from a dated source.
- **Why (in view, no P&L).** `hv4-BIG-X` and `hv4-CON-X` were recorded NOT_DECIDABLE_5PCT, final (QP-RULING-1101-hv4-reads: 140 of 220 and 155 of 364 attempts in the §1.4 class; the public feed logged a `slot_jump` data-loss gap about every 28 s). The scorer was not called and no P&L exists. This amendment was motivated by those verdicts. QP chose a complete getBlock source for NEW books (option B); old books are not moved (§4.1 (E) of that ruling).
- **Takes effect** on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict on the PR, and before the clock of any book it covers.

1. **Scope.** A book is covered if and only if its `bots.json` line and notebook copy (§1.2) name the tip source of item 2 as its feed, as §1.1 allows ("A book may read another live feed instead of this tape"). A covered book has a NEW `bot_id` (§1.2), its own P0, H and clock, and counts in N from its clock (Amendment 3 item 3). No book on the public tape is covered or changed. HV4, FLOOR, Q50, s1 and every registered book keep their feed, pins, gap rules and caps.
2. **The source, pinned per registration.** The registration record states, as path@sha256 or blob sha, every one of:
   - the adapter `tools/arena_tip_adapter.py`; the vendored reader `tools/arena_tip_reader.py` (a byte copy of `tools/r4b_tip_reader.py` under a non-r4b name, §3 item 1; blob `78ad185744147173d4e1036f35268012fcdcb066` at the time of writing); the writer `tools/arena_tip_tape_writer.py`; the pusher and the receiver; the hour-health tool and its thresholds file (item 5);
   - the history start H0 (item 7), the history window W, `--feed-delay-slots` d with its measurement date, the transport (QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader §3: the writer on fast-0, the derived copy pushed to the engine host, or the engine on the writer's host) and the derived copy's paths. The derived copy must not be on the walk-2 walker's filesystem (§3 item 8; `/data/mal` is on `/`, 71% used at the ruling): it lives on /data2;
   - d = ceil((p90 of the follower's `block_lag_ms_p90` samples + the p90 writer ingest lag + the p90 transport lag) / seconds per slot), from at least 24 h of the same window (`feed_delay_slots` in the adapter). HV4's 7 is not reusable.

   **Book-side and follower-side pins.** A change to a book-side pin (adapter, vendored reader, writer, pusher and receiver, hour-health tool and thresholds file, d, W, H0, transport, derived copy path) is a new `bot_id` with a new clock (§1.2). Follower-side values (the three decoder blobs, `trade_event_v`, the install tree, the unit's ExecStart flags including `--backlog-slots`, `--max-keep-days` and `--trade-event-v`, and the last start instant) are recorded at registration; the decoder blobs, `trade_event_v` and status freshness are checked live and fail closed; a follower restart on the same tree and flags is a recorded event (item 7); a change of tree, flags or decoder during a counted window needs a quant-proof note before it and is disclosed in every read it touches, as §1.4 treats the feed's pins.
3. **§1.4 gap kinds of the tip source.** The gap source of a covered book is the `gap` rows of the writer's output: the adapter's rows built from the follower's `gaps.jsonl` and the reader's own findings, and the writer's own rows (item 7). Each is one row `type gap`, `data_loss` true, `src` "tip", `from_slot`, `to_slot`, `missed_slots`, `[from_ms, to_ms]`, and a kind built as `tip_gap_` plus the reason code, never truncated (QP on #1165 R2; the exact strings are those the pinned shas emit and the registration lists them from the code). The engine takes them through the same `on_gap` and `_exclusion` path as the feed's (`tools/arena_paper.py`, unchanged), so a trade whose decision-to-exit span overlaps one, of its own program or of program `all`, is a §1.4 feed-gap exclusion counted in the 5% cap. The prog of each code, as the code at `ca9c2822` assigns it:
   | reason codes | prog |
   |---|---|
   | `backlog_jump`, `unfetchable`, `skip_contradicted`, `skip_contradicted_refetch`, `tip_bad_line`, `hour_file_missing`, `unparsable`, and the writer's `reader_problem`, `status_unhealthy`, `writer_hole` | `all`; counts for every program |
   | `unresolved_dropped`, `mixed_tx_index` | `pumpswap` |
   | `bad_row_tx_index`, `bad_row_fields` of a trade with a known venue | that trade's venue program (`pumpswap`, or `pumpfun` for a bonding trade) |
   | `bad_row_venue`, `bad_row_slot`, `bad_row_kind`, `bad_row_migration_type`, `bad_row_fields` of a create or migration row | `all` |
   A `gaps.jsonl` row with no `reason` takes its `kind` as the reason code (the follower writes its `unresolved_dropped` rows this way; they are prog `pumpswap` per the table), or `unknown` when it has neither (prog `all`). The code is the broader side for a PumpSwap book: the text states what the engine excludes.
   - **Not data loss:** skipped slots (the chain had no block): no row, no gap, no engine counter.
   - **Rows without `tx_index`.** PumpSwap prints that come back through `resolve_unresolved` carry none (the walker's rows do too). They pass through, are ordered by the engine's receive form (slot, 1, `t_recv_ms`, signature, `event_index`), which is deterministic here because the follower stamps one `t_recv_ms` on every row of a slot, and are never dropped or marked as loss. Only the order of two or more transactions of one pool in one slot is imprecise (an order imprecision, not data loss). A (pool, slot) group that mixes the two forms cannot come from the decoder; if one appears the adapter writes a `pumpswap` data_loss gap there. The counts `rows_no_tx_index`, `pool_slots_no_tx_index`, `pool_slots_multi_sig` and `pool_slots_mixed` are in the writer's hourly counts log and every read discloses them. QP's trigger: `pool_slots_multi_sig` above 1 an hour outside the first hour after a follower start, in the item 8 smoke, means QP re-rules before any clock.
   - **`pool_create` and pool age.** A pool whose creation is not in the host's history (created outside a migration, or migrated before the history's first hour H0) has no `pool_create` row, so `created_known` is false; HV4's `_age` then uses the time since the pool was first seen, a lower bound, so the pool passes a minimum-age check once seen long enough and a maximum-age check while first seen less than that maximum ago. A short history therefore widens an age-bounded universe. A covered book whose rule reads pool age meets item 7's history rule and states its handling in its registration (QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader §2: HV7 admits only `created_known` pools).
   - **Schema delta against the public tape** (pinned by `test_the_output_delta_against_the_feed_is_exactly_the_documented_one`): the tip rows are the walker's slim rows, so PumpSwap `pool_quote_amount`, `lp_fee`, `protocol_fee`, `creator_fee`, `mint_source` (and `supply_raw` on some sells) and, on bonding rows, `real_quote_reserve`, `real_base_reserve`, `protocol_fee_bps`, `protocol_fee`, `creator`, `creator_fee_bps`, `creator_fee` are absent; `tx_index` and `block_time` are added on data rows (except the rows above), `src` on gap rows. A `complete` or `pool_create` row comes before the trades of its slot, and a gap row's `to_ms` can equal the `t_recv_ms` of rows of the same slot.
4. **Fees and bot inputs (QP on #1165, round 2, item 5, option (a)).** No pricing or scoring code reads a row fee field (`tools/arena_paper.py`, `arena_stack.py`, `arena_score.py`). Fees come from the pinned constants of §1.4. Only the bot's view changes: `ArenaState.pumpswap_post` falls back to constant product without the LP fee (re-anchored at every print), so a book's state price and candles differ slightly from the public tape's. Binding consequences: (i) a covered book's P0, proofs and equivalence are run on this source; (ii) its registration lists the state fields its rule reads, with item 8's meaning of "needs"; (iii) item 3's schema delta is copied into the registration. **Parity set (§1.1).** The committed arena parity set and its §1.4 pins are kept for covered books: pricing reads fixed fee tables, reserves and V only, and the tip rows are in the arena tape's format plus two keys.
5. **Hour health for the tip source (replaces Amendment 6 item 2.1's tape-hour inputs for covered books; items 2.2 to 2.7 and item 3 apply unchanged).** `tools/arena_hour_health.py` reads tape-hour files. Covered books use the counts-log tool below whatever the transport (under transport B the derived copy does hold tape-hour files, but they are not the tool's input). A covered book pins, as path@sha256 before the clock, an outcome-blind per-hour tool that reads only (a) the writer's hourly counts log, (b) the follower-state sample file, (c) the writer's status (liveness), and (d) under transport B, the receiver's arrival and verification log. It may not read a tip row, a ledger, a fill, a price, an exit, a position, P&L, a decision-row content or any count of decisions (Amendment 6 item 2.1's forbidden list). It prints no mint, wallet or signature. The values below are FIXED by quant-proof (QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader §5), before any sampler figure was seen; they never change on a counted window (Amendment 6 item 2.3), a change before the smoke needs a new quant-proof ruling with its reason, and none is allowed after the smoke starts.
   | key | value | label | definition (per UTC hour h) |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `writer_hour_complete` | 1 (required) | price-path | The counts-log line for h exists, written by the registered writer sha256s. No writer restart in h. No gap > 60 s between writer heartbeats overlapping h. The derived hour h is closed and, under transport B, verified on the engine host within 15 min after h ends. |
   | `max_loss_span_s` | 120 | price-path | The longest data_loss span (`to_ms - from_ms`, unclipped) among gap rows of the book's programs or prog `all` that overlap h. |
   | `max_data_silence_s` | 60 | price-path | The longest interval between consecutive data rows of each program the book trades, by the follower's `t_recv_ms`, carried across hour boundaries and unclipped (from the counts log). |
   | `status_bad_samples` | 0 | price-path | Follower-state samples inside h with `decoder_blobs_pinned` not true, `trade_event_v` not true, or `ts_ms` older than 30 s at the sample instant. |
   | `min_samples_per_hour` | 50 | price-path | The window sampler runs at a pinned 60 s period, as a host-side MiScusi job chained across its time limit. |
   | `max_sample_gap_s` | 180 | price-path | The longest gap between consecutive samples, clipped to h. |
   | `max_input_age_s` | 30 | decision-input | At each minute :02 of h: the instant minus the follower `t_recv_ms` of the newest data row of the book's programs available to the **engine host's view** at that instant (under transport B, as verified per the receiver's arrival log; the receiver is a transport process, so Amendment 6 item 2.2 allows its stamp). |

   The thresholds file is pinned as canonical JSON: `{"max_data_silence_s":{"label":"price-path","value":60},"max_input_age_s":{"label":"decision-input","value":30},"max_loss_span_s":{"label":"price-path","value":120},"max_sample_gap_s":{"label":"price-path","value":180},"min_samples_per_hour":{"label":"price-path","value":50},"status_bad_samples":{"label":"price-path","value":0},"writer_hour_complete":{"label":"price-path","value":1}}`. Report-only, never excluding: rows per minute, catch-up duration, ingest lag, transport lag p50 and p90, writer and receiver RSS, dropped rows by code, the four no-`tx_index` counts. A data_loss gap shorter than `max_loss_span_s` does not exclude an hour; it excludes by trade under §1.4 (item 3). Amendment 6 item 3's 10% cap and §1.4's 5% cap are unchanged and separate. The per-hour table and its sha256 go to the notebook at least daily (Amendment 6 item 2.6).
6. **Pre-clock feed bar (QP-RULING-1101-hv4-reads §4.4).** Before the clock of a covered book, a records line states, over the 24 closed UTC hours before the clock and per program the book trades, the count of data_loss gap rows of that program or of prog `all` in the registered writer's counts log, the writer running at its registered sha256s for all 24 hours (a prog `all` row counts for both programs); lambda = that count per second; and the bar 1 - e^(-lambda x T_max) <= 0.025, where T_max is the book's registered time-stop or maximum hold. A book with T_max above 0.6076 h (-24 h x ln 0.975) passes only with ZERO such rows; at or below that, one row in 24 h passes (0.02469 at 0.6 h). HV7's T_max is 1 h, so one row gives 0.0408 and fails. On a fail the clock slips to the next 00:00Z, never a stop by itself, and no N is spent. The bar uses counts and a constant of the book's own rule only.
7. **Warm-up, history, decisions, restarts. For covered books this item REPLACES §3 item 6's last clause ("a book that reads another live feed uses only data from its own clock on"); it is an amendment of that clause, not a clarification, because that clause plainly forbids pre-clock state.**
   - **History window W = 70 h.** The writer's `TipReader` floor is max(2026-10-16T01, its start hour - 70 h) (the constructor's `catch_up_hours` and `lookback_h`; the vendored bytes are unchanged). The live dir keeps 72 h, so the start leaves at least 2 h of margin. **H0** is the first hour of the derived copy's continuous history written by the pinned writer. Every hour a covered book reads, warm-up included, is written by the writer at the registered sha256s; a writer change starts a new derived copy with a new H0.
   - **History rule.** The clock of a covered book is at or after H0 + L, where L is the longest age bound or look-back its rule reads (HV7: 7 d). An earlier clock with a narrower universe is not allowed: the counted window must test the registered universe from its first hour.
   - **Warm-up and decisions.** Catch-up rows are state only: the bots get no call on them. Decisions and counted attempts are made only after the reader reports `caught_up`, only from the clock, and only on rows of hours at or after 2026-11-01T00:00Z. Until 2026-11-14T00:00Z, no decision within [s0, s0 + 360 s) of a pool whose s0 is before 2026-11-01T00:00Z (EXP-024 §3 Look-2, from any source). No hour before 2026-10-16T01 is ever read (D2/D3/D4 stay), and no copy of the tip tape other than the live dir (read only by the pinned writer) and the writer's derived copy (read only by the readers of item 9) is ever read: the archive and every other copy stay closed.
   - **Unhealthy means a gap row, with no grace period ("the pinned time" is 0 s).** A latched or problem reader makes the writer write a data_loss gap row (prog `all`, kind `tip_gap_reader_problem`) over [last healthy instant, recovery instant]; a failed status check (the three decoder blobs, `decoder_blobs_pinned`, `trade_event_v`, `ts_ms` not older than 30 s) does the same with kind `tip_gap_status_unhealthy`. A latched reader stops the writer. A hole the writer cannot cover after its own restart is a data_loss gap, kind `tip_gap_writer_hole`.
   - **Restarts.** A restart of the writer or of the engine host is `engine_restart` for open positions (Amendment 6 item 4 unchanged). A restart of the follower on the same tree and flags is a recorded event (LAB_STATE row 115, `date -u`) and does not end a covered book (item 2); it is never made by a covered book's lane, and by nobody before the r4-b DRY run's end E = 2026-11-07T03:00Z. A planned follower change inside a counted window needs a quant-proof note before it; a safety stop does not.
8. **Per book, before the clock** (in addition to Amendment 3 item 1, Amendment 4 item 2 and Amendment 6 item 1):
   - a new `bot_id`;
   - **P0:** on at least 3 full closed UTC days inside the derived copy's history (H0 onward, at the registered writer), the latest 3 before the registration, with H from Amendment 4 item 2.2. A P0 day with history shorter than L is a declared undercount;
   - **Smoke:** at least 24 h on this source at the final `frozen_sha` (replacing Amendment 6 item 1.3's 2 h; real model for an LLM book), meeting item 6, reporting dropped-row counts by code, anon RSS peak, rows per minute, the catch-up duration, the feed-delay measurement, and the follower's `lag_slots` and `backlog_jumps` for at least 1 h before the start, during catch-up and during live tailing, with every tip reader running (the r4-b DRY host and the writer);
   - **Equivalence:** the book's own decision-equivalence on this source at its final sha, run on the WRITER's output built from exploration walker hours (the adapter's synthetic `FrozenOrders` proof, config md5 `5579235fd66e7a69bf56a09c8ee5d7f7`, proves the adapter and no book). The public-tape proof and P0 tools keep their bytes; the tip-source variants are new pinned files;
   - **State fields:** the registration lists every state field the rule, screen, cards and vetoes read, `creator` and `creator_net_sol` in `hv4_cards` included. "Needs" a missing key means reading a key, or a state field, that is always absent or None on this source; the constant-product post-trade price is the source's view, not a need (QP round 2 on #1165, item 5 (ii), clarified). A field that is always None on this source must be shown, by code and a test, not to change a decision, or the book is not eligible;
   - **Age:** a book whose rule reads pool age states its handling (item 3; HV7: only `created_known` pools, by an HV7-side guard, with HV4's `_age`, `filters` and `hv6_cards` untouched);
   - T_max from the code, and a check that the clock is not in a kill-review week (LAB_STATE "Now").
9. **Seals and disclosures.**
   - **Readers.** The tip files are read only by the follower, its daily archive job, the r4-b DRY host and the writer named in QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader. The writer's derived copy is a derived copy of the sealed tip tape under every tip seal, and is read only by: the writer, its pusher and receiver, the pinned tip-source runner of a covered book and its smoke at the registered frozen sha, the pinned tip-source P0 counter (counts only), and a later C0 decision host at the same sha. No other copy exists (no backup, no mirror back, no Console copy). A covered book's ledgers and decision rows are sealed like every other arena book's. No join of a covered book's output to r4-b's decision log before EXP-027's read.
   - **Domain seals.** D2, D3 and D4 (§3 item 6) cover the tip tape's copies of [2026-10-02T10, 2026-10-16T01) (QP-RULING-1031-r4b-history §2.1 reading); they do not make other tip hours D2 hours.
   - **Walk-2 information and outcomes.** The tip tape is walk-2-hour information (DEC-030 §2 R1), a near byte-copy of walk 2's content (same decoder, getBlock, real `tx_index` and `block_time`). Outcomes of covered books over Forward walk 2 calendar hours, EXP-027's confirmation window [2026-11-07T06, B - 1 h] included, are walk-2-hour OUTCOME information (DEC-030 §2 R1). They are never computed on hours of [2026-10-02T10, 2026-10-16T01). Every covered read discloses this, the counts of item 3, the migration-only `pool_create` and the age handling, the schema delta and the fee consequence of item 4, H0, W, and the transport with its lag.
   - **Order of the records.** Owed before the FIRST read of a tip file by any covered-book host (the writer), not before the clock: QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader; the SEALS-BRIEF entry with its AGENT-BRIEF-COMMON line; the HOLDOUT_LEDGER changelog line and the Forward walk 2 Status-cell note on the 2026-10-31 r4-b pattern; the LAB_STATE row 115 note with any read ACL. Owed before a covered clock: the writer, pusher, receiver and hour-health tools (own PRs, QP and reviewer), this amendment merged, and the per-book records of item 8.
10. **What does not change.** The promotion gate and every threshold; §2 (deciding cell, one read per `bot_id`, outcome-blind boundary, steps 1 to 4); §1.4's 5% cap, parity and pins; Amendment 3 item 2's c and item 3's multiplicity block; Amendment 6 items 2.2 to 2.7, 3 and 4; every seal except the one clause item 7 replaces for covered books; the ban on a live send (§4); and every registered book's feed, pins and clock. Amendment 16 (HV7 as a sibling family and its budget split) is unchanged and is not this amendment.

*Provenance.* Sources: `/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/manager18/QP-RULING-1101-hv4-reads.md` (sha256 `c6e94e747634079bc4b0cd4d79d2f30319edce7b4abc808d1bf9919a9e126073`); `/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/manager18/QP-RULING-1101-arena-tip-reader.md` (sha256 `91dcb0d7feaaef42590ef3661913d3debff6b9597d0520d6a3d19b904c72e1ec`; the QP verdict is PR #1166 comment 6552888187 and the reviewer's is comment 6552790468); `/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/reports/2026-11-01/qp-1165.md` (rounds 1 and 2; sha256 `a4c0a401512dcf67edac9da0728946126b45443e34502fff8acf5c36f15dcaae`; the round 2 text is also PR #1165 comment 6552601341); `docs/design/arena-tip-adapter.md` at `ca9c2822`; and QP-RULING-1031-r4b-history §2.1. **Not measured by this amendment:** the tip's data-loss rate, the share of rows without `tx_index` on the tip (the exploration proxy is a cold-cache hour: 861 PumpSwap rows in 2026-08-16T12), the writer's throughput or memory, transport lag, any smoke, P0 or outcome. **Owed:** see item 9 (order of the records). QP OK and reviewer verdict: on the PR at its merged head.

### Amendment 25 (DRAFT; HV4-T, and FLOOR on the tip source not registered; number = the next free DEC-031 amendment at merge: Amendment 16 is held by open PR #1019, Amendment 23 Erratum 1 by #1169, the Amendment 21 item 1.5 stop record by #1175 and the s1t draft by #1186; a renumber at merge is a fill; first written 2026-11-01T22:56:10Z, revised 2026-11-01T23:20:43Z from `date -u` on mal-research-0 on reviewer comment 6565358726 (R1, R4, R5, R6, N3), with Part B moved to #1186; records only; before the registration, P0 use, smoke, `bots.json` line and clock of every book it covers; outcome-blind; status: DRAFT until merge, QP OK on the exact head and a reviewer verdict)
DEC-031 Amendment 25: Part A registers HV4's nine lane-G arms at HV4's frozen sha as new books on the fast-0 tip source (`hv4t-`), covered books of Amendment 23. Part C records that FLOOR is not re-registered on the tip source. Design and evidence: `docs/design/arena-tip-cohort.md` (same PR). No tape, tip file, derived-copy `tape-*` file, counts log, ledger, `bots.json` line, decision row, outcome, fill, exit or P&L was opened to write it. **No book has cleared the promotion gate. Nothing here is a profit claim.**
- **In view (no P&L):**
  - `hv4-BIG-X` and `hv4-CON-X` NOT_DECIDABLE_5PCT (QP-RULING-1101-hv4-reads, sha256 `c6e94e747634079bc4b0cd4d79d2f30319edce7b4abc808d1bf9919a9e126073`);
  - the owner's word of 2026-11-01 withdrawing the HV4 successors' API spend (the Amendment 21 item 1.5 stop record, #1175);
  - the manager's plan to redirect that budget to tip-source books (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/manager18/PUSH-PLAN-1101.md`, lane L1);
  - lane L5's desk audit (`/data/mal/hunt-1008/ops/reports/2026-11-02/L5-fastest-read.md`);
  - the state-field listing of PR #1180 (two decision changes, item 7).
  No HV4 sibling verdict was in view.
- **Takes effect** on merge, after quant-proof's OK on the exact final head and a reviewer verdict, and before the clock of any book it covers.
- **Not push-family.** Every book here is a DEC-031 arena book: "Arena books are not push-family books. They take no DEC-030 slot and do not change EXP-027's k" (§3 item 3). The books count in this DEC's N from their clocks (Amendment 23 item 1). Nothing here registers into DEC-030's slot or DEC-014's push family.

**Part A: HV4-T.**
1. **The books.**
   - `bot_id`s: `hv4t-CON-L`, `hv4t-CON-F`, `hv4t-CON-P`, `hv4t-CON-X`, `hv4t-CON-R`, `hv4t-BIG-L`, `hv4t-BIG-F`, `hv4t-BIG-X`, `hv4t-BIG-R`.
   - `frozen_sha` `9ed4c5ff63935696b2a89150e13565e123047754`, with HV4's arms, prompts, reviewer instructions, sampling settings, deciding stake 0.25 and deciding cell; lane G only.
   - Model ids: `["claude-haiku-5-5"]` on the L, F, P and R arms, `[]` on the X arms.
   - Stack config pin: sha256 `1e611a5a636dfe6bd563ab5258f6b498cdb8906500ee8bf2f0264a2621621986`.
   - They are new books, not successors: nothing of Amendment 21 item 1 carries to them. They are covered books of Amendment 23 (item 1).
2. **Source and feed delay.**
   - Amendment 23 item 2's pins apply.
   - The derived copy is `/data2/mal/arena-tip-feed/hv7` on mal-research-0 (transport B), passed as `--tape-dir`. It is the one copy, shared by every covered book; no second copy is made.
   - `--feed-delay-slots` d is measured and pinned per Amendment 23 item 2, with its date. HV4's 7 is not used.
   - H0 is taken from the writer's counts log at registration.
3. **Identity and roots** (Amendment 21 item 1.2 pattern).
   - Each book has its own `--root`, `--ledger-root` and `--registry` on /data2, fixed in the registration record. None of them is ever `/data/mal/arena/haiku-v4`, `/data/mal/arena/ledger` or `/data/mal/arena/bots.json`.
   - The frozen code registers `hv4-<arm>` in the own registry. The arena registry `/data/mal/arena/bots.json` gets the nine `hv4t-<arm>` lines through `register_bot`.
   - The map is pinned in both the notebook copy and a pushed commit. If the two differ, there is no clock.
   - Every job passes these paths explicitly and refuses HV4's.
   - **No HV4-T process (smoke or runner) is alive at the start of HV4's segment-2 job #1562.** Its start guard refuses while any `hv4_runner.py` process is alive. So each HV4-T job refuses to start before 2026-11-07T12:30:00Z, and until the job list shows #1562 running or ended.
   - HV4's jobs are never changed for HV4-T (§4).
4. **Age and history** (Amendment 23 items 3 and 7).
   - **The frozen code.** It reads 365 min of tape before its start (`ArenaState` `WARM_MIN_DEFAULT`). It evicts a key that has been idle more than 6 h. It ages a pool whose creation it has not seen from the pool's first sight (`hv4_cards._age`). So the age band of 2 h to 7 d is judged on the runner's own view, and it admits pools older than 7 d while they were first seen less than 7 d ago in that view.
   - **Disclosed universe differences from HV4's run on the public tape:**
     - (a) On the tip, `pool_create` exists for migration pools only (Amendment 23 item 3). So a pool created outside a migration has no creation row, fails `age_min` until it has been seen for 2 h, and is aged from first sight (#1180 scenario S3).
     - (b) On the tip, bonding rows carry no `creator`. So the creator is known only from a create row inside the runner's view (#1180 scenario S2; item 7).
   - **History rule.** Under Amendment 23 item 7, L = 7 d, the longest age bound the rule reads. With H0 = 2026-10-29T09:00:00Z (receiver status `history_from`, to be confirmed from the counts log at registration), H0 + 7 d = 2026-11-05T09:00:00Z, which is before the clock of item 6. So the history rule is met.
   - The runner itself still reads only its 365-min warm-up; that is disclosed, not an amendment of item 7.
5. **P0 and H.**
   - **Reuse.** HV4's own P0 is reused, as Amendment 21 item 1.1 did: the A2 count at `9ed4c5ff` on 2026-10-21 to 10-23 (registration record section 3a). Its lowest arm is 230.667 would-be attempts a day, an upper bound, with no day at 0.
   - **Why it replaces the tip P0.** For these books it replaces two clauses: Amendment 23 item 1's "its own P0" and item 8's tip P0 (see Unchanged). Every tip day before 2026-11-08 lies in HV4's counted hours, and a structure count of the HV4 rule there is HV4 attempt-count information under HV4's seal.
   - **H.** H = 14 days (Amendment 4 item 2.2): 14 for any rate of at least 10.714285714285714.
   - **Disclosed:** the count is on the public tape, 18 or more days before the clock.
   - **Tip-side floor.** It is fixed in a notebook entry before the smoke and checked on the smoke's decision rows (item 7).
   - **If quant-proof refuses this reuse.** P0 is then a tip count with `tools/hv4_p0_count.py` (sha256 `3e2539d063af712e7b8db085f62c5543c91fa0e03233087a10ac71756a88c4c7`) on 3 full closed UTC days in [2026-11-08, clock). Its fixed-first text is notebooked before the count. The clock is then not before 2026-11-12T00:00:00Z.
6. **Clock, window and the HV4 seal's end.**
   - **The seal's end.** The SEALS-BRIEF HV4 line reads "No pricing or retune of any HV4 rule on counted hours (>= 2026-10-25T00Z)", with no end. This amendment reads "counted hours" as HV4's counted window [2026-10-25T00:00:00Z, 2026-11-08T00:00:00Z). HV4 counts no entry from 1794096000000 (Amendment 15 item 1), and its confirmation ends there too (Amendment 15 item 3).
   - **Under that reading,** HV4-T's smoke and runner price the HV4 rule only on hours at or after 2026-11-08T00:00:00Z. Quant-proof confirms the reading. The manager writes it into the SEALS-BRIEF HV4 line before the smoke, beside item 7's reader line.
   - **Clock.** `clock_start_ms` **1794268800000 (2026-11-10T00:00:00Z)**, or the first 00:00Z after the last item of item 7. A late item is a slip, never a stop, and spends no N.
   - **An earlier clock** (2026-11-08T00Z or 2026-11-09T00Z) is not otherwise allowed. It needs a quant-proof ruling that a smoke at `9ed4c5ff`, in a never-opened scratch ledger on hours before 2026-11-08T00:00Z, is not "pricing ... of any HV4 rule on counted hours".
   - **Window.** H = 14 d. Entries at or after clock + 14 d do not count: 1795478400000 (2026-11-24T00:00:00Z) for the 11-10 clock.
   - **END.** The runner's END is that window end plus 13 h 05 min: 1795525500000 (2026-11-24T13:05:00Z). The runner is after-chained in two segments, with the hand-off instant pinned in the registration record. The hand-off is a planned restart under Amendment 6 item 4.
   - HV4's counted window and confirmation end at 1794096000000, so the windows do not overlap (Amendment 3 item 5).
7. **Before the clock.** These are in addition to Amendment 3 item 1, Amendment 4 item 2, Amendment 6 item 1 and Amendment 23 items 6 and 8:
   - **Seals.** The SEALS-BRIEF tip entry names the HV4-T runner and smoke at `9ed4c5ff` as readers of the derived copy, and the SEALS-BRIEF HV4 line carries item 6's end reading. Both need a quant-proof note and the manager's edit, before the smoke.
   - **State fields** (Amendment 23 item 8). PR #1180 lists them on synthetic rows. No field is None on the tip in every scenario, but two fields are sometimes None and change a decision. Each needs a quant-proof ruling (a disclosure only, a guard at a new sha, or not eligible) before registration:
     - (S2) when the mint's create row is outside the runner's view, `creator` is None, so `sell_route` counts the creator as a non-creator seller (the feed vetoes, the tip enters);
     - (S3) a pool created outside a migration fails `age_min` until it has been seen for 2 h.
   - **Equivalence** (Amendment 23 item 8). HV4's runner in sim at `9ed4c5ff`, StubModel on the LLM arms, over the WRITER's output built from explore-0814 walker hours, against the same runner on the adapter's in-memory stream. The decision-log md5 must be equal. A minimum-decisions floor is notebooked first.
   - **Smoke.** At least 24 h at `9ed4c5ff` with the real model, on hours at or after 2026-11-08T00:00:00Z, with Amendment 23 item 8's reports and the tip-side P0 floor of item 5.
     - It runs beside HV4's #1562 until 2026-11-08T13:05:00Z: two `hv4_runner.py` processes at one sha, on separate roots, about 2 x 45,000 MB. Quant-proof rules this is allowed (R-BESIDE), and the memory check of Amendment 21 item 1.4 (e) is applied at the smoke's start as well as at the runner's.
   - **Hour health.** Amendment 23 item 5's tool, thresholds and sampler, for program `pumpswap`.
   - **Feed bar.** Amendment 23 item 6's line with T_max = 12 h (BIG `max_hold_min` 720). So there must be ZERO `pumpswap` or `all` data_loss rows in the 24 h before the clock.
   - **Calendar.** The kill-review-week check (LAB_STATE "Now" lists none).
8. **Read.**
   - The rule: Amendment 15 items 1, 2, 3, 6 and 7, re-based to the HV4-T clock and window end exactly as Amendment 21 item 1.3 re-bases them. Records are named `hv4t-`; multiplicity uses the arena-wide source; the Amendment 6 hour table comes from Amendment 23 item 5's tool.
   - The read tools are copies of `tools/hv4_read_instant.py` and `tools/hv4_am15_read.py` whose diff shows only those changes. They merge with quant-proof's OK and a reviewer verdict before the earliest B (2026-11-15T00:00:00Z for the 11-10 clock).
   - No count, preview or read runs before they merge.
9. **Spend.**
   - **Authority.** The API authority for these books is the owner's 2026-10-28 API line (notebook `n_8Yfl--RzLCx2Dw`, as quoted in `docs/rules/hv4.md`: "API spend past 11-11 is OK ... API spend goes to the Haiku TRADER only"; the line Amendment 21 item 1.4 (g) cites) plus this amendment.
   - **Caps.** The pinned caps of `tools/hv4_arms.py` apply per HV4-T root. Lane G is 5 LLM-arm ledgers x 0.50 + RISK 1.00 + 3 reviewer ledgers x 0.10 = **3.80 USD a day at cap**. That is at most 57.00 USD over 14 days and the run-out day, plus at most 3.80 for the smoke.
   - **Budget.** This is inside the 4.90 USD a day the owner withdrew from the successors, which the manager's plan (PUSH-PLAN-1101 L1) redirects here. The owner's recorded words withdraw that spend and name no new use, so this amendment does not say the owner redirected it.
   - **Guards.** The 160 USD month guard applies, with the owner's 200 USD console limit as backstop.
   - **Chain calls.** The pinned caps of `tools/hv4_chain.py` apply. Helius credits are reported per job.
10. **Seals.**
    - HV4's seal applies to each `hv4t-` book, root, ledger root and registry, from registration until its read is recorded (Amendment 21 item 1.3 seal text, with `hv4t-`).
    - No HV4-T output is joined to any HV4 output before the last HV4 read is recorded.
    - Amendment 23 item 9 applies in full.
11. **N and disclosures.** N + 9 at the clock (Amendment 4 item 2.5). Every HV4-T read prints:
    - the nine HV4 verdicts and the successor stop record;
    - that HV4-T runs the rule HV4 ran, on another source, and that any later selection between the two is in-sample;
    - item 4's age semantics and its universe differences (a) and (b), with quant-proof's rulings on S2 and S3;
    - item 5's P0 reuse and item 6's seal reading;
    - the Amendment 23 item 9 disclosures;
    - that these books were chosen with `hv4-BIG-X` and `hv4-CON-X` NOT_DECIDABLE_5PCT in view (no P&L).
12. **C0.** Out of scope. A C0 of an HV4-T book needs its own recorded PASS, DEC-032, and a later text for the bridge's ids and source.

**Part C: FLOOR on the tip source is not registered.**
- `l1-floor-v2` was never registered (Amendment 9 item 2: the A2 rate was 6.0 a day, below 12.5; N was not spent).
- A tip-source FLOOR book is not registered now, and N is not spent. The reasons:
  - At the rates in view (6.0 and 4.6 a day, public tape), 100 attempts need about 17 to 22 days. That is beyond Amendment 9's 12-day H and beyond one MiScusi job.
  - Its 24 h holds meet about 0.37 tip data-loss gaps a day (lane L5). That puts about 1 - e^(-0.37) = 0.31 of trades in the §1.4 class, against the 5% cap.
- A revival needs its own amendment, a tip gap-fill, an in-clock hand-off and a tip P0.

**Unchanged:**
- the promotion gate and every threshold (n >= 100; >= 5 UTC dates with a majority positive; CI90 lower bound > 0, 1,000 draws, seed 1, 5th percentile; ex-top-3 > 0; flat 15% and pressure-fail at slope scale 1);
- §2; §1.4's 5% cap; Amendment 3 items 2 and 3; Amendment 6;
- Amendment 23, except, for the `hv4t-` books only, item 1's "its own P0" and item 8's tip P0, which item 5 replaces (item 7's history rule applies with L = 7 d);
- HV4's books, jobs and reads;
- Q50, s1 and EXP-024 (this amendment does not touch them; s1t is #1186);
- the ban on a live send (§4).
